{"parentUuid":null,"isSidechain":true,"promptId":"4cc8afad-ad11-43a6-b5c0-6e6b0bd41ce6","agentId":"a2755cdc18585e64b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":"Review target: `ONLY ON THE OBSERVATORY MODULE not on the entire system.`\n\n`minimal prompt → single careful diff pass → ≤15 findings`\n\nYou are reviewing a pull request for real bugs. Run `git diff @{upstream}...HEAD` (or `git diff main...HEAD` / `git diff HEAD~1`\nif there's no upstream) to get the unified diff under review. If there are\nuncommitted changes, or the range diff is empty, also run `git diff HEAD` and\ninclude the working-tree changes in scope — the review often runs before the\ncommit. If a PR number, branch name, or file path was passed as an argument,\nreview that target instead. Treat this diff as the review scope.\n\nReview the diff as a careful senior engineer would: read every hunk, open the surrounding files for context as needed (Read, Grep, git log/blame/show), and hunt for correctness issues — wrong or inverted conditions, off-by-one, null/undefined dereference, missing `await`, dropped error handling, removed guards or validations, broken callers of changed functions, races. Prefer real failure modes over style; every finding needs a concrete scenario in which the code misbehaves.\n\nWhen you are done, submit at most 15 findings via the ReportFindings tool, filling its fields as defined — for each: the file path and start line, a severity, and a comment that states the issue and the concrete scenario in which the code misbehaves. Quality over quantity: include everything you genuinely believe is a real issue, and nothing you don't.\n\nAfter the tool call, also restate the findings in your final reply — one line each, `file:line — summary` — so they stay visible in sessions that do not render tool output.\n"},"isMeta":true,"uuid":"50980750-546a-43b6-b76e-435d90b9b548","timestamp":"2026-08-04T10:24:10.565Z","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/usr/share/pac/dev/projects/mt-radar/node-dash","sessionId":"ce1ab9c9-df2f-4f79-a39d-d17da291a371","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"feat/alarm-transport-plugin","slug":"elegant-swimming-wirth"}

Runs /idiot with the finding's citations as the mandatory read list. Audit resolution is done externally via scripts/audit_ref.py — never mark a finding resolved.\n- codebox: Output text in a plain code block suitable for copy and paste. No markdown formatting, no syntax highlighting, no explanations.\n- dp-help: /dp-help — DaisyPug Component Lookup\n- esp-mqtt: Send MQTT commands to ESP32 knock detector and print responses.\n- esp-validate: Validate an ESPHome YAML config without compiling. Fast schema/syntax check that doesn't pollute the build directory.\n- esp-ws: Connect to ESP32 WebSocket API and stream log/status/event messages.\n- git-deploy: Commit (if needed), push, git pull on a remote host, and optionally restart a service.\n- hallucinate: Stop seeing what you expect instead of what's actually there. Re-read every source, quote literally, never describe from memory.\n- idiot: Five-phase workflow: Investigate, Document, Implement, Observe, Track. Each phase requires user approval before proceeding. Replaces /idi.\n- investigate: Read-only investigation of a bug or issue. FORBIDDEN from changing any files. Reads code, traces logic, reports findings.\n- ltc4150: Drive the LTC4150 coulomb counter at http://192.168.10.181 to measure real current draw and battery life of a device. Use whenever a power/battery question needs a number instead of an estimate — \"how long will this run\", \"is it sleeping\", \"what does the camera cost\". ALWAYS read GET /sys?a=help and /sys?a=schema first — the device publishes its own CI-guarded contract. This skill adds the measurement discipline that contract cannot: how the instrument misleads you if read naively.\n- mesh-trends: Interrogate the node-dash store for real trends, infer causes, and return the top 5 things worth investigating — with the controls that stop each one being an artefact.\n- mt-chat: /mt-chat — Meshtastic chat via MCP\n- my-skills: List all custom skills alphabetically with descriptions.\n- no-argue: Accept the user's report as fact. Activate /hallucinate + /investigate rules and find the root cause.\n- no-guessing: Two strikes and you /idiot. Activates strict 2-attempt limit — if both fail, STOP and switch to /idiot (Investigate, Document, Implement, Observe, Track) workflow.\n- pacmon: Establish a serial monitor to a remote host where an ESP32 device is connected.\n- park: Peter just thought of something mid-task and does NOT want you to stop. Capture it as an mcpp task to investigate later, keep doing the current work without pausing, and surface the parked list when you next go idle. Invoke on 'park this', 'just thought of', 'don't stop but', 'add a task to look at X and carry on', or any aside dropped while other work is in flight.\n- playwright: /playwright — Browser Testing via MCP Playwright\n- proc: Reminder: mandatory mt-radar procedure — /idi + mcpp-plan + mcpp-git from correct directory. Invoke when Claude skips or shortcuts the workflow.\n- rak-comms: Talk to the RAK4631 — read what it is actually doing, and flash it — through the one tool that owns the USB link. Use whenever a unit is not answering, before theorising from mesh-side logs, and for EVERY flash. Replaces the old rak-debug skill.\n- rak-debug: Attach the RAK4631 serial console and read what the device is actually doing — receive/transmit state, wake windows, PKI, replies. Use whenever a unit is not answering, before theorising from mesh-side logs. Covers the exact command that works from a tool call, the HB field meanings, and the flash hazards.\n- schematic: Validate bom.yaml + netlist.yaml and regenerate SVG schematic using netlistsvg.\n- session-id: Name or rename the current session for easy resuming later with the cr script.\n- ta2m-chat: Chat with Peter over the private mesh channel while he is remote, via our own pac-host API. Listen for his messages and reply over the radio.\n- task-new: Create a new mcpp-plan task with goal, plan, steps, and notes — all in one go.\n- terse: Standing terseness rule for the REST OF THE SESSION. Invoke when replies have grown long, are recapping, or are explaining things that were not asked. Does not lapse after one answer.\n- tune-test: /tune-test — Proper A/B tuning test on Pi\n- undo: Revert Claude's most recent file changes using memory, without git commands.\n- xlead: Coordinate two agent sessions over xsession with ONE of them leading. Use when work spans two repos and both sessions could code the same thing — a leader issues gated work orders, the follower does nothing until told, and completion is reported with evidence. Prevents the two failure modes that actually happen: both sessions burning tokens on the same problem, and each guessing at the other's code.\n- xsession: Coordinate with agent sessions in other repos over a shared board — ask questions that must not be dropped, answer theirs, and stay reachable. Folder-per-channel, cursor-tracked. Helps with aesthetic direction, typography, and making choices that don't read as templated defaults.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Serves this user's live capability roster and the typed call definitions. Load it whenever the user asks for an artifact needing any such runtime behavior.\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". 'packet' : 'nodedb',\n385\t+        lead,\n386\t+        lead_reason,\n387\t+      };\n388\t+    });\n389\t+\n390\t+  return { rows, limit: Number(limit) || 15, window_days: Number(windowDays) || 0, record_km: record };\n391\t+}\n392\t+\n393\t+/** WHAT HAS THIS ENGINE ACTUALLY PRODUCED? — the CONTRIBUTION band.\n394\t+ *\n395\t+ *  Peter, 2026-08-04: \"but is it actually doing anything ?!\" and, when the\n396\t+ *  answer turned out to be a shell query I had run myself, \"and why is all of\n397\t+ *  that ONLY visible to you ?\"\n398\t+ *\n399\t+ *  That was the real defect. That page already states how it knows a thing — \"assumed\",\n238\t+   *  \"device-reported\", \"inferred from silence\" — and the board needs exactly the\n239\t+   *  same discipline. No new visual language was invented; there was no need.\n240\t+   *\n241\t+   *  The desc is the SERVER's basis string. The browser assembles no explanation\n242\t+   *  (BROWSER_CONTRACT) and this method picks no thresholds — it colours what it\n243\t+   *  is told. */\n244\t+  obsVerdictClass(state) {\n245\t+    return state === 'ok'    ? 'text-success'\n246\t+         : state === 'warn'  ? 'text-warning'\n247\t+         : state === 'fault' ? 'text-error'\n248\t+         : 'text-base-content/60';\n249\t+  },\n250\t+\n251\t+  /** A sparkline: shape only. No axes, no gridlines, no legend — those live on\n252\t+   *  Charts, which is where you go once the shape makes you suspicious. Peter:\n253\t+   *  \"one glance is better with graphical rather than text, but thats not to say\n254\t+   *  we should litter it with graphs as we have chart page to drill down.\"\n255\t+   *\n256\t+   *  A null value BREAKS the line rather than plotting zero: a gap reads as \"not\n257\t+   *  known\", a zero reads as \"nothing happened\". Same rule as the bands chart. */\n258\t+  obsSpark(series, state) {\n259\t+    const pts = (series || []);\n260\t+    if (!pts.length) return '';\n261\t+    const vals = pts.map(p => p.v).filter(v => v != null);\n262\t+    if (!vals.length) return '';\n263\t+    const W = 200, H = 34;\n264\t+    const max = Math.max(...vals), min = Math.min(...vals);\n265\t+    const span = (max - min) || 1;\n266\t+    const X = i => pts.length === 1 ? W / 2 : (i * W) / (pts.length - 1);\n267\t+    const Y = v => H - 3 - ((v - min) / span) * (H - 8);\n268\t+    const colour = state === 'ok' ? 'oklch(var(--su))'\n269\t+                 : state === 'warn' ? 'oklch(var(--wa))'\n270\t+                 : state === 'fault' ? 'oklch(var(--er))'\n271\t+                 : 'oklch(var(--bc)/0.4)';\n272\t+    const seg = [];\n273\t+    let open = false;\n274\t+    pts.forEach((p, i) => {\n275\t+      if (p.v == null) { open = false; return; }\n276\t+      seg.push(`${open ? 'L' : 'M'}${X(i).toFixed(1)} ${Y(p.v).toFixed(1)}`);\n277\t+      open = true;\n278\t+    });\n279\t+    const last = [...pts].reverse().find(p => p.v != null);\n280\t+    const li = pts.indexOf(last);\n281\t+    return `<path d=\"${seg.join(' ')}\" fill=\"none\" stroke=\"${colour}\" stroke-width=\"1.6\"/>`\n282\t+         + (li >= 0 ? `<circle cx=\"${X(li).toFixed(1)}\" cy=\"${Y(last.v).toFixed(1)}\" r=\"2.2\" fill=\"${colour}\"/>` : '');\n283\t+  },\n284\t+\n285\t+  /** THE FRONTIER RULER — the one glance-graphic that is a GAP, not a trend.\n286\t+   *  A sparkline cannot say \"92 of 189, and the notch is over there\", and that\n287\t+   *  gap is the entire product. Also honest in a way the bare record never was:\n288\t+   *  189.1 km alone implied success for a month. */\n289\t+  /** THE FRONTIER RULER — positions only, no markup.\n290\t+   *\n291\t+   *  This used to build an SVG string containing both the bar and its labels.\n292\t+   *  The SVG was declared `viewBox=\"0 0 520 40\"` and rendered 1309 px wide, so\n293\t+   *  everything inside it scaled 2.52x: `font-size: 11` painted at 27.7 px,\n294\t+   *  larger than the biggest stat value on the page, and the two value labels\n295\t+   *  overlapped into an unreadable smear because they were positioned in\n296\t+   *  520-unit space with no knowledge of how wide they would actually paint.\n297\t+   *\n298\t+   *  Peter has now called this out twice — the bands chart first (\"why are the\n299\t+   *  fonts so huge, that new chart bears no resemblence id design\"), then this.\n300\t+   *  So the rule, not just the instance: TEXT DOES NOT GO INSIDE A STRETCHED\n301\t+   *  SVG. Geometry scales, which is what SVG is for; type must not, because it\n302\t+   *  belongs to the page's type ramp.\n303\t+   *\n304\t+   *  What is left here is arithmetic. The markup lives in the template, where\n305\t+   *  the labels are ordinary HTML at `text-xs` and cannot collide.\n306\t+   *\n307\t+   *  Returns percentages, because the bar is a responsive HTML element now and\n308\t+   *  has no fixed coordinate space to map into. */\n309\t+  // DISPLAY ARITHMETIC ONLY — the same licence the radar has. Every judgement\n310\t+  // (tone, hit_rate, direct, snr_db) arrives decided from boardVerdicts(); this\n311\t+  // turns those into pixels and nothing else. It must not classify, bin or\n312\t+  // threshold — if that is ever needed it belongs server-side.\n313\t+  // SCALE TO THE DATA, NOT THE RECORD. This used to be\n314\t+  // max(record_km, best_km) * 1.08, which pinned the record near the right edge\n315\t+  // BY CONSTRUCTION — 189.1 landed at 92.6% of a 204 km axis and read as \"the\n316\t+  // end of the line\". Worse, 27 nodes lie BEYOND the record (furthest 245.2 km)\n317\t+  // and the clamp stacked all 27 into one pile on the right edge, and the 250\n318\t+  // tick never rendered because 250 > 204.\n319\t+  //\n320\t+  // Peter, 2026-08-03: \"it is not against time, 189km is still at the end\".\n321\t+  // Correct, and it was my scaling, not the labelling.\n322\t+  //\n323\t+  // The axis is now the 250 km plausibility ceiling that boardVerdicts() already\n324\t+  // filters on, so the axis and the payload agree by construction and nothing\n325\t+  // clamps. Beyond 250 km positions are MQTT-sourced and not RF evidence.\n326\t+  obsStripSpan() {\n327\t+    const f = this.charts?.board?.frontier;\n328\t+    const furthest = Array.isArray(f?.strip) && f.strip.length\n329\t+      ? f.strip[f.strip.length - 1].km   // server sorts ascending by km\n330\t+      : 0;\n331\t+    return Math.max(250, furthest, f?.record_km ?? 0) || 1;\n332\t+  },\n333\t+\n334\t+  // TIME on x, DISTANCE on y — the axes the card should always have had.\n335\t+  // Display arithmetic only: the server decides the window (from_ts/to_ts), what\n336\t+  // counts as answered, and whether a signal reading is honest.\n337\t+  obsReachPoints() {\n338\t+    const f = this.charts?.board?.frontier;\n339\t+    const pts = f?.reach;\n340\t+    if (!Array.isArray(pts) || !pts.length) return [];\n341\t+    const t0 = f.from_ts, t1 = f.to_ts;\n342\t+    const tSpan = Math.max(1, t1 - t0);\n343\t+    const yMax = this.obsReachYMax();\n344\t+    return pts.map(p => ({\n345\t+      left:   +Math.max(0, Math.min(100, ((p.ts - t0) / tSpan) * 100)).toFixed(3),\n346\t+      bottom: +Math.max(0, Math.min(100, (p.km / yMax) * 100)).toFixed(3),\n347\t+      direct: p.direct,\n348\t+      // Server-decided class; the browser only maps it to a colour.\n349\t+      // Served since 283f556 — the fallback classified here, which is the\n350\t+      // browser deciding. If `kind` is ever missing that is a payload bug and\n351\t+      // should be visible, not silently papered over.\n352\t+      kind: p.kind ?? null,\n353\t+      // Signal opacity across the LoRa demod range, -20 to +12 dB. Normalising a\n354\t+      // server-supplied number, not deciding what counts as strong.\n355\t+      opacity: p.snr_db == null ? null : Math.max(0.4, Math.min(1, (p.snr_db + 20) / 32)),\n356\t+      title: `${p.first_ever ? 'FIRST EVER CONTACT · ' : ''}${p.label} · ${p.km} km · ${new Date(p.ts * 1000).toLocaleString()}`\n357\t+           + (p.snr_db == null ? ' · no direct signal' : ` · ${p.snr_db} dB direct`),\n358\t+    }));\n359\t+  },\n360\t+\n361\t+  // y must include the RECORD even when the window never got near it — the gap\n362\t+  // to that line is the entire point of the card.\n363\t+  obsReachYMax() {\n364\t+    const f = this.charts?.board?.frontier;\n365\t+    const furthest = Array.isArray(f?.reach) && f.reach.length\n366\t+      ? Math.max(...f.reach.map(p => p.km)) : 0;\n367\t+    return Math.max(f?.record_km ?? 0, furthest, 10) * 1.1;\n368\t+  },\n369\t+\n370\t+  // y scale in km. Round steps chosen from the axis height so the labels stay\n371\t+  // readable rather than tracking the data exactly.\n372\t+  obsReachYTicks() {\n373\t+    const yMax = this.obsReachYMax();\n374\t+    const step = yMax > 150 ? 50 : yMax > 60 ? 25 : 10;\n375\t+    const out = [];\n376\t+    for (let km = step; km < yMax; km += step) {\n377\t+      out.push({ km, bottom: +((km / yMax) * 100).toFixed(3) });\n378\t+    }\n379\t+    return out;\n380\t+  },\n381\t+\n382\t+  // Horizontal reference lines: the all-time record, and the window's best.\n383\t+  obsReachLines() {\n384\t+    const f = this.charts?.board?.frontier;\n385\t+    if (!f) return [];\n386\t+    const yMax = this.obsReachYMax();\n387\t+    const best = f.best_km ?? f.window_km;\n388\t+    const at = km => +Math.max(0, Math.min(100, (km / yMax) * 100)).toFixed(3);\n389\t+    const out = [];\n390\t+    if (f.record_km != null) out.push({ kind: 'record', bottom: at(f.record_km), km: f.record_km, text: `${f.record_km} km record` });\n391\t+    if (best != null)        out.push({ kind: 'best',   bottom: at(best),        km: best,        text: `${best} km best` });\n392\t+    return out;\n393\t+  },\n394\t+\n395\t+  // Day boundaries down the x axis, so \"when\" is readable.\n396\t+  obsReachDays() {\n397\t+    const f = this.charts?.board?.frontier;\n398\t+    if (!f?.from_ts) return [];\n399\t+    const t0 = f.from_ts, t1 = f.to_ts, span = Math.max(1, t1 - t0);\n400\t+    const out = [];\n401\t+    const d = new Date(t0 * 1000); d.setHours(0, 0, 0, 0); d.setDate(d.getDate() + 1);\n402\t+    for (let g = d.getTime() / 1000; g < t1; g += 86400) {\n403\t+      out.push({ left: +(((g - t0) / span) * 100).toFixed(3),\n404\t+                 text: new Date(g * 1000).toLocaleDateString(undefined, { day: 'numeric', month: 'short' }) });\n405\t+    }\n406\t+    return out;\n407\t+  },\n408\t+\n409\t+  obsStripBars() {\n410\t+    const f = this.charts?.board?.frontier;\n411\t+    const strip = f?.strip;\n412\t+    if (!Array.isArray(strip) || !strip.length) return [];\n413\t+    const span = this.obsStripSpan();\n414\t+    return strip.map(e => {\n415\t+      // A 0% bar must still be VISIBLE — it marks a node we placed and tried,\n416\t+      // which is different from empty axis. Never-tried (null) sits at the floor\n417\t+      // too, distinguished by tone, not by height.\n418\t+      const h = e.hit_rate == null ? 8 : Math.max(8, Math.min(100, e.hit_rate));\n419\t+      // Signal opacity ramps across the LoRa demod range: -20 dB floor to +12 dB.\n420\t+      // Normalising a server-supplied number, not deciding what counts as strong.\n421\t+      const op = e.snr_db == null ? null\n422\t+               : Math.max(0.35, Math.min(1, (e.snr_db + 20) / 32));\n423\t+      return {\n424\t+        left: +Math.max(0, Math.min(100, (e.km / span) * 100)).toFixed(3),\n425\t+        height: +h.toFixed(1),\n426\t+        tone: e.tone,\n427\t+        opacity: op,\n428\t+        title: `${e.label} · ${e.km} km`\n429\t+             + (e.hit_rate == null ? ' · never tried' : ` · ${e.hit_rate}% of ${e.attempts}`)\n430\t+             + (e.snr_db == null ? ' · no direct signal' : ` · ${e.snr_db} dB direct`),\n431\t+      };\n432\t+    });\n433\t+  },\n434\t+\n435\t+  // Axis ticks at round distances, only as far as the span reaches.\n436\t+  obsStripTicks() {\n437\t+    const span = this.obsStripSpan();\n438\t+    return [0, 50, 100, 150, 200, 250]\n439\t+      .filter(km => km <= span)\n440\t+      .map(km => ({ km, left: +((km / span) * 100).toFixed(3) }));\n441\t+  },\n442\t+\n443\t+  obsWindowLabel() {\n444\t+    const d = this.charts?.board?.frontier?.window_days;\n445\t+    if (d == null) return '';\n446\t+    return d === 0 ? 'all time' : `last ${d} day${d === 1 ? '' : 's'}`;\n447\t+  },\n448\t+\n449\t+  obsFrontierPct() {\n450\t+    const f = this.charts?.board?.frontier;\n451\t+    if (!f || f.record_km == null) return { window: 0, record: 0 };\n452\t+    // ONE SPAN, SHARED. This previously carried a comment saying it used the\n453\t+    // same span as the bars while the line beneath it computed a second one —\n454\t+    // so after the bars moved to the 250 km axis the notches stayed on the old\n455\t+    // 204 km scale and pointed at the wrong nodes. The comment was right and the\n456\t+    // code was wrong; now it calls the helper instead of restating it.\n457\t+    // best_km is window_km's correct name; the alias is read only until the\n458\t+    // payload drops it.\n459\t+    const best = f.best_km ?? f.window_km;\n460\t+    const span = this.obsStripSpan();\n461\t+    const pct  = km => Math.max(0, Math.min(100, (km / span) * 100));\n462\t+    return {\n463\t+      window: best == null ? 0 : +pct(best).toFixed(2),\n464\t+      record: +pct(f.record_km).toFixed(2),\n465\t+    };\n466\t+  },\n467\t+\n468\t+  /** THE SELECTION FUNNEL — where the candidate pool actually goes.\n469\t+   *\n470\t+   *  Peter, 2026-08-03: \"I need to be able to see what you see in raw data, but\n471\t+   *  I want to see it at a glance.\"\n472\t+   *\n473\t+   *  This is the single most informative thing read from the database all day\n474\t+   *  that was not on screen. It explains why a pool of 531 nodes produces a\n475\t+   *  shortlist of 3, and EVERY SEGMENT NAMES THE KNOB THAT CAUSED IT — so the\n476\t+   *  answer to \"why is it only trying three things\" is on the page rather than\n477\t+   *  in a query.\n478\t+   *\n479\t+   *  The old line showed 3 of the 5 exclusions and omitted the two LARGEST\n480\t+   *  (beyond-window 191, silent 151), which is how the biggest filters stayed\n481\t+   *  invisible.\n482\t+   *\n483\t+   *  Server-computed counts; this only lays them out. */\n484\t+  obsFunnel() {\n485\t+    const s = this.missions?.summary;\n486\t+    if (!s) return [];\n487\t+    const segs = [\n488\t+      { k: 'shown',      n: s.shown ?? 0,                 label: 'working now', tone: 'bg-primary' },\n489\t+      { k: 'cooling',    n: s.skipped_cooling ?? 0,       label: `cooling · ${s.cooldown_min}m`,        tone: 'bg-info/60' },\n490\t+      { k: 'exhausted',  n: s.skipped_exhausted ?? 0,     label: `tried out · ${s.attempts_per_target}`, tone: 'bg-base-content/25' },\n491\t+      { k: 'window',     n: s.skipped_beyond_window ?? 0, label: `too far · ${s.window_km}km step`,      tone: 'bg-warning/70' },\n492\t+      { k: 'silent',     n: s.skipped_silent ?? 0,        label: `silent · ${s.max_silence_days}d`,      tone: 'bg-base-content/40' },\n493\t+      // WAS LABELLED \"no position\", WHICH IT NEVER WAS. skipped_suspect counts\n494\t+      // km > ceiling_km (250) — implausible COMPUTED distances — and every one\n495\t+      // of those nodes HAS a position. The wrong label sent the number to Peter\n496\t+      // as a position problem twice. The server now publishes ceiling_km so this\n497\t+      // states the server's rule instead of inventing a meaning for it.\n498\t+      { k: 'suspect',    n: s.skipped_suspect ?? 0,       label: `beyond ${s.ceiling_km ?? 250}km · unverifiable`, tone: 'bg-base-content/15' },\n499\t+      // NO CLASS CLAIMED IT — attempted, no reply, nearer than the bar. These\n500\t+      // left the selector uncounted until 2026-08-03, and they are why the\n501\t+      // shortlist empties without saying so.\n502\t+      { k: 'unclassified', n: s.skipped_unclassified ?? 0, label: `no rule matched · under ${Math.round(s.bar_km ?? 0)}km`, tone: 'bg-error/40' },\n503\t+      // NEVER REACHED THE SELECTOR AT ALL — filtered by the evidence query\n504\t+      // before any counter existed. The largest exclusion, and it was absent\n505\t+      // from this bar entirely.\n506\t+      { k: 'unpositioned', n: s.unpositioned ?? 0,        label: 'no position at all',                   tone: 'bg-base-content/10' },\n507\t+    ].filter(x => x.n > 0);\n508\t+    const total = segs.reduce((a, b) => a + b.n, 0) || 1;\n509\t+    return segs.map(x => ({ ...x, pct: (100 * x.n) / total, total }));\n510\t+  },\n511\t+\n512\t+  /** Set the ANALYSIS WINDOW. Server state, not a browser preference — Peter,\n513\t+   *  2026-08-03: \"the browser is a presentation layer ONLY, this filtering must\n514\t+   *  be done backend so anyone using the browser sees the same thing.\"\n515\t+   *\n516\t+   *  So this PUTs and does nothing else. The server re-broadcasts `charts` and\n517\t+   *  every viewer re-renders from that — no optimistic local state, the same\n518\t+   *  rule that governs the target crosshair above. The allowed values are the\n519\t+   *  server's enum; a value outside it is rejected with 400 rather than clamped. */\n520\t+  async setObsWindow(days) {\n521\t+    try {\n522\t+      await fetch('/config/observatory.window_days', {\n523\t+        method: 'PUT',\n524\t+        headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' },\n525\t+        body: JSON.stringify({ value: Number(days) }),\n526\t+      });\n527\t+    } catch (e) {\n528\t+      console.error('window set failed', e);\n529\t+    }\n530\t   },\n531\t \n532\t   /** Pin a mission to the top of the queue, or unpin it.\n533\tdiff --git a/public/partials/tab-observatory.html b/public/partials/tab-observatory.html\n534\tindex 10cad92..7c9832b 100644\n535\t--- a/public/partials/tab-observatory.html\n536\t+++ b/public/partials/tab-observatory.html\n537\t@@ -24,55 +24,329 @@\n538\t   <div class=\"tabs tabs-bordered w-full sm:w-fit shrink-0\">\n539\t     <a class=\"tab px-3 sm:px-5 text-xs sm:text-sm\" :class=\"obsTab==='board' && 'tab-active font-semibold'\"\n540\t        @click=\"switchObsTab('board')\">Board</a>\n541\t+    <a class=\"tab px-3 sm:px-5 text-xs sm:text-sm\" :class=\"obsTab==='leads' && 'tab-active font-semibold'\"\n542\t+       @click=\"switchObsTab('leads')\">Leads</a>\n543\t     <a class=\"tab px-3 sm:px-5 text-xs sm:text-sm\" :class=\"obsTab==='radar' && 'tab-active font-semibold'\"\n544\t        @click=\"switchObsTab('radar')\">Radar</a>\n545\t     <a class=\"tab px-3 sm:px-5 text-xs sm:text-sm\" :class=\"obsTab==='map' && 'tab-active font-semibold'\"\n546\t        @click=\"switchObsTab('map')\">Map</a>\n547\t     <a class=\"tab px-3 sm:px-5 text-xs sm:text-sm\" :class=\"obsTab==='charts' && 'tab-active font-semibold'\"\n548\t        @click=\"switchObsTab('charts')\">Charts</a>\n549\t+    <a class=\"tab px-3 sm:px-5 text-xs sm:text-sm\" :class=\"obsTab==='logs' && 'tab-active font-semibold'\"\n550\t+       @click=\"switchObsTab('logs')\">Logs</a>\n551\t     <a class=\"tab px-3 sm:px-5 text-xs sm:text-sm\" :class=\"obsTab==='receptions' && 'tab-active font-semibold'\"\n552\t        @click=\"switchObsTab('receptions')\">Receptions</a>\n553\t   </div>\n554\t \n555\t+  <!-- ══════════════════════ LEADS ══════════════════════════════════════════ -->\n556\t+  <!-- The STATE band of docs/APP_ROLE.md: node-dash's own data, summarised, so a\n557\t+       lead can be seen and acted on without switching screens. ADDED beside\n558\t+       Board, which is untouched — Peter: \"that is a good refrence we should keep\n559\t+       both\".\n560\t+\n561\t+       THIS TABLE DECIDES NOTHING. lead, lead_reason, heard_tone, role_tone,\n562\t+       can_be_door and evidence all arrive served from nodeTable(). The markup\n563\t+       maps a tone to a class and prints the rest. No threshold appears here. -->\n564\t+  <div x-show=\"obsTab==='leads'\" class=\"flex-1 min-h-0 flex flex-col gap-3\"\n565\t+       x-effect=\"obsTab==='leads' && obsPinned === null && obsLoadPinned()\">\n566\t+    <div class=\"flex items-baseline gap-3 shrink-0\">\n567\t+      <h2 class=\"font-display text-sm tracking-widest uppercase text-primary\">Leads</h2>\n568\t+      <span class=\"text-xs text-base-content/50\"\n569\t+            x-text=\"charts?.nodes ? `${charts.nodes.rows.length} of the furthest live nodes · past ${charts.nodes.record_km} km is a lead · ${charts.nodes.window_days}-day window` : ''\"></span>\n570\t+      <span class=\"ml-auto text-xs text-base-content/40\">SOURCE: NODE-DASH · CONTEXT, NOT SCORE</span>\n571\t+    </div>\n572\t+\n573\t+    <!-- ARE WE GAINING GROUND? Every value served by contribution(); the page\n574\t+         decides nothing, including the verdict sentence and whether it is bad\n575\t+         news. Peter: \"why is all of that ONLY visible to you ?\" -->\n576\t+    <template x-if=\"charts?.contribution\">\n577\t+      <div class=\"shrink-0 rounded-box border p-3\"\n578\t+           :class=\"charts.contribution.gaining ? 'border-success/40 bg-success/5' : 'border-error/40 bg-error/5'\">\n579\t+        <div class=\"flex items-baseline gap-2 flex-wrap\">\n580\t+          <span class=\"font-display text-xs tracking-widest uppercase\"\n581\t+                :class=\"charts.contribution.gaining ? 'text-success' : 'text-error'\"\n582\t+                x-text=\"charts.contribution.gaining ? 'Gaining ground' : 'Not gaining ground'\"></span>\n583\t+          <span class=\"text-xs\" x-text=\"charts.contribution.verdict\"></span>\n584\t+          <span class=\"ml-auto text-[10px] text-base-content/40\"\n585\t+                x-text=\"'last ' + charts.contribution.window_days + ' days'\"></span>\n586\t+        </div>\n587\t+        <div class=\"flex gap-6 flex-wrap mt-2 text-xs\">\n588\t+          <span><span class=\"text-base-content/50\">furthest new route</span>\n589\t+            <b x-text=\"charts.contribution.furthest_km + ' km'\"></b>\n590\t+            <span class=\"text-base-content/50\" x-text=\"'(' + charts.contribution.furthest_label + ')'\"></span>\n591\t+            <span class=\"text-error\" x-show=\"charts.contribution.furthest_short_by > 0\"\n592\t+                  x-text=\"'— ' + charts.contribution.furthest_short_by + ' km short'\"></span></span>\n593\t+          <span><span class=\"text-base-content/50\">median new route</span>\n594\t+            <b x-text=\"charts.contribution.median_km + ' km'\"></b></span>\n595\t+          <span><span class=\"text-base-content/50\">cost</span>\n596\t+            <b x-text=\"charts.contribution.cost_per_new_route\"></b>\n597\t+            <span class=\"text-base-content/50\">attempts per new node</span></span>\n598\t+          <span><span class=\"text-base-content/50\">new nodes</span>\n599\t+            <b x-text=\"charts.contribution.new_routes\"></b>\n600\t+            <span class=\"text-base-content/40\"\n601\t+                  x-text=\"charts.contribution.new_routes_unplaced ? '(' + charts.contribution.new_routes_unplaced + ' unplaceable)' : ''\"></span></span>\n602\t+        </div>\n603\t+        <div class=\"mt-2 pt-2 border-t border-base-content/10 text-[11px] text-base-content/60\"\n604\t+             x-show=\"!charts.contribution.gaining\">\n605\t+          <span class=\"text-base-content/40\">why not further —</span>\n606\t+          <span x-text=\"'strategy ' + charts.contribution.caps.strategy\"></span> ·\n607\t+          <span x-text=\"'window ' + charts.contribution.caps.window_km + ' km above a ' + charts.contribution.caps.bar_km + ' km bar'\"></span> ·\n608\t+          <span x-text=\"'ceiling ' + charts.contribution.caps.ceiling_km + ' km'\"></span> ·\n609\t+          <span class=\"text-warning\" x-text=\"charts.contribution.caps.skipped_beyond_window + ' skipped beyond window'\"></span> ·\n610\t+          <span class=\"text-warning\" x-text=\"charts.contribution.caps.skipped_silent + ' skipped as silent (' + charts.contribution.caps.max_silence_days + 'd)'\"></span>\n611\t+        </div>\n612\t+      </div>\n613\t+    </template>\n614\t+\n615\t+    <div class=\"overflow-auto flex-1 min-h-0 rounded-box border border-base-300/60\">\n616\t+      <table class=\"table table-xs table-pin-rows\">\n617\t+        <thead><tr>\n618\t+          <th>Node</th><th>Where</th><th class=\"text-right\">km</th><th class=\"text-right\">az</th>\n619\t+          <th>Role</th><th class=\"text-right\">hops</th><th class=\"text-right\">heard</th>\n620\t+          <th class=\"text-right\">SNR</th><th class=\"text-right\">probes</th>\n621\t+          <th>Evidence</th><th class=\"text-right\">Act</th>\n622\t+        </tr></thead>\n623\t+        <tbody>\n624\t+          <template x-for=\"n in (charts?.nodes?.rows || [])\" :key=\"n.num\">\n625\t+            <tr :class=\"n.lead && 'bg-primary/5'\">\n626\t+              <td class=\"font-mono whitespace-nowrap\">\n627\t+                <span :class=\"n.lead ? 'text-primary font-semibold' : ''\" x-text=\"n.label\"></span>\n628\t+              </td>\n629\t+              <td class=\"text-base-content/60 max-w-[13rem] truncate\" x-text=\"n.place || '—'\"\n630\t+                  :title=\"n.lead_reason || ''\"></td>\n631\t+              <td class=\"text-right font-mono\" x-text=\"n.km\"></td>\n632\t+              <td class=\"text-right font-mono text-base-content/50\" x-text=\"n.az != null ? n.az + '°' : '—'\"></td>\n633\t+              <td class=\"whitespace-nowrap\">\n634\t+                <span x-show=\"n.role\" class=\"badge badge-xs\" :class=\"toneCls(n.role_tone)\" x-text=\"n.role\"></span>\n635\t+                <span x-show=\"!n.role\" class=\"text-base-content/30\">—</span>\n636\t+                <span x-show=\"!n.can_be_door\" class=\"text-[9px] text-base-content/40 ml-1\">never a door</span>\n637\t+              </td>\n638\t+              <td class=\"text-right font-mono\" x-text=\"n.hops ?? '—'\"></td>\n639\t+              <td class=\"text-right font-mono\" :class=\"toneCls(n.heard_tone)\" x-text=\"n.heard\"></td>\n640\t+              <td class=\"text-right font-mono text-base-content/60\" x-text=\"n.snr != null ? n.snr : '—'\"></td>\n641\t+              <td class=\"text-right font-mono\"\n642\t+                  :class=\"n.probes === 0 ? 'text-base-content/30' : ''\"\n643\t+                  x-text=\"n.answered > 0 ? n.probes + '/' + n.answered : n.probes\"></td>\n644\t+              <td>\n645\t+                <span class=\"badge badge-xs\"\n646\t+                      :class=\"n.evidence === 'packet' ? 'badge-success' : 'badge-ghost'\"\n647\t+                      x-text=\"n.evidence === 'packet' ? 'packet' : 'nodedb'\"></span>\n648\t+              </td>\n649\t+              <td class=\"text-right whitespace-nowrap\">\n650\t+                <!-- Only the two actions that are actually wired. A dead button\n651\t+                     teaches the operator that the page lies; probe and analyse\n652\t+                     are not built and so are not drawn. -->\n653\t+                <button class=\"btn btn-xs btn-ghost\" @click=\"obsPin(n.num)\"\n654\t+                        :class=\"obsPinned === n.num && 'text-warning'\"\n655\t+                        :title=\"obsPinned === n.num ? 'pinned — click to unpin' : 'pin: work this ahead of everything, past the ceiling/silence/window filters'\"\n656\t+                        x-text=\"obsPinned === n.num ? 'pinned' : 'pin'\"></button>\n657\t+              </td>\n658\t+            </tr>\n659\t+          </template>\n660\t+        </tbody>\n661\t+      </table>\n662\t+      <div x-show=\"!(charts?.nodes?.rows || []).length\" class=\"p-6 text-center text-base-content/40 text-sm\">\n663\t+        No live placed nodes in the window.\n664\t+      </div>\n665\t+    </div>\n666\t+    <div class=\"text-xs text-base-content/40 shrink-0\">\n667\t+      Highlighted rows are leads — past the record, alive, and unworked. Hover a place for the reason.\n668\t+      <span class=\"text-base-content/30\">probe and analyse are not built yet, so they are not shown.</span>\n669\t+    </div>\n670\t+  </div>\n671\t+\n672\t   <!-- ══════════════════════ BOARD ══════════════════════════════════════════ -->\n673\t   <div x-show=\"obsTab==='board'\" class=\"flex-1 min-h-0 flex flex-col gap-3\">\n674\t \n675\t+    <!-- THE FRONTIER RULER. The only glance-graphic here that is a GAP rather\n676\t+         than a trend, and the gap is the whole product: a sparkline cannot say\n677\t+         \"92 of 189, and the notch is over there\". -->\n678\t+    <template x-if=\"charts?.board?.frontier?.record_km\">\n679\t+      <div class=\"card bg-base-100 shadow shrink-0\">\n680\t+        <div class=\"card-body p-3 gap-1\">\n681\t+          <h2 class=\"text-xs font-display font-bold tracking-widest uppercase text-base-content/60\">\n682\t+            Frontier\n683\t+            <span class=\"font-normal normal-case tracking-normal text-base-content/40\">— how far we reached, over time</span>\n684\t+          </h2>\n685\t+\n686\t+          <!-- TIME on x, DISTANCE on y. Peter said so in his first message\n687\t+               about this card and I built a distance ruler twice instead. A\n688\t+               ruler cannot answer \"is the frontier rising?\", and it parks the\n689\t+               all-time record near the right edge purely because the record is a\n690\t+               big NUMBER — which reads as recent. Against time, the 4 Jul record\n691\t+               is a CEILING drawn across the plot and the gap below it is the\n692\t+               work left. -->\n693\t+          <div class=\"relative h-24 my-1 border-l border-b border-base-content/15\">\n694\t+            <!-- y SCALE. Without it the height of a dot is unreadable — the whole\n695\t+                 point of putting distance on y. -->\n696\t+            <template x-for=\"(y, yi) in obsReachYTicks()\" :key=\"'y'+yi\">\n697\t+              <div class=\"absolute inset-x-0 h-px bg-base-content/8\" :style=\"`bottom:${y.bottom}%`\"></div>\n698\t+            </template>\n699\t+            <template x-for=\"(y, yi) in obsReachYTicks()\" :key=\"'yl'+yi\">\n700\t+              <span class=\"absolute left-1 -translate-y-1/2 font-mono text-[9px] text-base-content/30\"\n701\t+                    :style=\"`bottom:${y.bottom}%`\" x-text=\"y.km + ' km'\"></span>\n702\t+            </template>\n703\t+\n704\t+            <template x-for=\"(d, di) in obsReachDays()\" :key=\"'d'+di\">\n705\t+              <div class=\"absolute inset-y-0 w-px bg-base-content/8\" :style=\"`left:${d.left}%`\"></div>\n706\t+            </template>\n707\t+\n708\t+            <template x-for=\"(l, li) in obsReachLines()\" :key=\"'l'+li\">\n709\t+              <div class=\"absolute inset-x-0 h-px\"\n710\t+                   :class=\"l.kind === 'record' ? 'bg-warning/70' : 'bg-primary/50'\"\n711\t+                   :style=\"`bottom:${l.bottom}%`\" :title=\"l.text\"></div>\n712\t+            </template>\n713\t+            <!-- The two reference labels sit ~6% apart on a 96 px plot, which is\n714\t+                 ~6 px — drawn on the same side they overlapped into an unreadable\n715\t+                 smear. Record rides ABOVE its line, best BELOW its own, so they\n716\t+                 separate by construction however close the values get. -->\n717\t+            <template x-for=\"(l, li) in obsReachLines()\" :key=\"'lt'+li\">\n718\t+              <span class=\"absolute right-1 font-mono text-[10px] px-1 rounded bg-base-100/80\"\n719\t+                    :class=\"l.kind === 'record' ? 'text-warning -translate-y-full' : 'text-primary translate-y-full'\"\n720\t+                    :style=\"`bottom:${l.bottom}%`\" x-text=\"l.text\"></span>\n721\t+            </template>\n722\t+\n723\t+            <!-- BARS, not plotted points. Peter: \"i said bars not plotted\n724\t+                 points\". Each bar stands at WHEN it happened and rises to HOW FAR\n725\t+                 it reached, so the silhouette is the reach over the window and\n726\t+                 the record line is the ceiling above it. -->\n727\t+            <template x-for=\"(p, pi) in obsReachPoints()\" :key=\"'p'+pi\">\n728\t+              <div class=\"absolute bottom-0 -translate-x-1/2 rounded-t-sm\"\n729\t+                   :class=\"{ 'bg-warning w-[4px] z-10': p.kind === 'new',\n730\t+                             'bg-success w-[3px]':      p.kind === 'direct',\n731\t+                             'bg-primary/60 w-[3px]':   p.kind === 'relay' }\"\n732\t+                   :style=\"`left:${p.left}%;height:${p.bottom}%` + (p.opacity != null ? `;opacity:${p.opacity}` : '')\"\n733\t+                   :title=\"p.title\"></div>\n734\t+            </template>\n735\t+          </div>\n736\t+\n737\t+          <div class=\"relative h-3 font-mono text-[10px] text-base-content/35\">\n738\t+            <template x-for=\"(d, di) in obsReachDays()\" :key=\"'x'+di\">\n739\t+              <span class=\"absolute -translate-x-1/2\" :style=\"`left:${d.left}%`\" x-text=\"d.text\"></span>\n740\t+            </template>\n741\t+          </div>\n742\t+\n743\t+          <div class=\"flex justify-between items-center font-mono text-xs text-base-content/40\">\n744\t+            <span x-text=\"obsWindowLabel()\"></span>\n745\t+            <span class=\"flex items-center gap-2 text-[10px]\">\n746\t+              <span class=\"flex items-center gap-1\"><i class=\"inline-block w-2 h-3 rounded-sm bg-warning\"></i>NEW — first ever contact</span>\n747\t+              <span class=\"flex items-center gap-1\"><i class=\"inline-block w-2 h-3 rounded-sm bg-success\"></i>heard direct — opacity is signal</span>\n748\t+              <span class=\"flex items-center gap-1\"><i class=\"inline-block w-2 h-3 rounded-sm bg-primary/60\"></i>answered via relay</span>\n749\t+            </span>\n750\t+            <!-- The legend named the ruler encodings (hit rate,\n751\t+                 tried / never tried). This plot has neither: one dot per ANSWERED\n752\t+                 traceroute, y is how far it reached. -->\n753\t+            <span class=\"text-base-content/30\">each bar = one answered traceroute</span>\n754\t+          </div>\n755\t+        </div>\n756\t+      </div>\n757\t+    </template>\n758\t+\n759\t+    <!-- THE VERDICTS. Peter, 2026-08-03: \"at first glance it has been identical\n760\t+         since it was first built, I see no progress and no evidence that anything\n761\t+         is even happening.\"\n762\t+\n763\t+         Three answers, not three totals: is it alive, is it producing, is it\n764\t+         getting further. Every state and every basis string is computed by the\n765\t+         server (BROWSER_CONTRACT) — this markup colours what it is told and\n766\t+         assembles no explanation of its own.\n767\t+\n768\t+         Liveness is deliberate. Peter uses the node page \"because I can see things\n769\t+         happening\", and what makes that page feel alive is that every number is\n770\t+         anchored to NOW — \"8m ago\", \"in 6m 23s\". So the loop card leads with a\n771\t+         ticking time-since, not an average. -->\n772\t+    <template x-if=\"charts?.board\">\n773\t+      <div class=\"grid grid-cols-1 sm:grid-cols-2 lg:grid-cols-4 gap-3 shrink-0\">\n774\t+\n775\t+        <div class=\"stat bg-base-100 rounded-lg shadow py-3 px-4\">\n776\t+          <div class=\"stat-title text-xs opacity-100 text-base-content/75\">Loop</div>\n777\t+          <svg viewBox=\"0 0 200 34\" class=\"w-full -mb-1\" x-html=\"obsSpark(charts.board.loop.series, charts.board.loop.state)\"></svg>\n778\t+          <div class=\"stat-value text-2xl font-mono tabular-nums\"\n779\t+               :class=\"obsVerdictClass(charts.board.loop.state)\"\n780\t+               x-text=\"charts.board.loop.since_sec == null ? '—'\n781\t+                       : charts.board.loop.since_sec < 90 ? charts.board.loop.since_sec + 's ago'\n782\t+                       : charts.board.loop.since_sec < 5400 ? Math.round(charts.board.loop.since_sec/60) + 'm ago'\n783\t+                       : Math.round(charts.board.loop.since_sec/3600) + 'h ago'\"></div>\n784\t+          <div class=\"stat-desc text-xs whitespace-normal leading-snug opacity-100 text-base-content/80\" x-text=\"charts.board.loop.basis\"></div>\n785\t+        </div>\n786\t+\n787\t+        <div class=\"stat bg-base-100 rounded-lg shadow py-3 px-4\">\n788\t+          <div class=\"stat-title text-xs opacity-100 text-base-content/75\">Yield</div>\n789\t+          <svg viewBox=\"0 0 200 34\" class=\"w-full -mb-1\" x-html=\"obsSpark(charts.board.yield.series, charts.board.yield.state)\"></svg>\n790\t+          <div class=\"stat-value text-2xl font-mono tabular-nums\"\n791\t+               :class=\"obsVerdictClass(charts.board.yield.state)\"\n792\t+               x-text=\"charts.board.yield.rate == null ? '—' : charts.board.yield.rate + '%'\"></div>\n793\t+          <div class=\"stat-desc text-xs whitespace-normal leading-snug opacity-100 text-base-content/80\" x-text=\"charts.board.yield.basis\"></div>\n794\t+        </div>\n795\t+\n796\t+        <div class=\"stat bg-base-100 rounded-lg shadow py-3 px-4\">\n797\t+          <div class=\"stat-title text-xs opacity-100 text-base-content/75\">Frontier</div>\n798\t+          <svg viewBox=\"0 0 200 34\" class=\"w-full -mb-1\" x-html=\"obsSpark(charts.board.frontier.series, charts.board.frontier.state)\"></svg>\n799\t+          <div class=\"stat-value text-2xl font-mono tabular-nums\"\n800\t+               :class=\"obsVerdictClass(charts.board.frontier.state)\"\n801\t+               x-text=\"charts.board.frontier.window_km == null ? '—' : charts.board.frontier.window_km + ' km'\"></div>\n802\t+          <div class=\"stat-desc text-xs whitespace-normal leading-snug opacity-100 text-base-content/80\" x-text=\"charts.board.frontier.where\"></div>\n803\t+          <div class=\"stat-desc text-xs whitespace-normal leading-snug opacity-100 text-base-content/60\" x-text=\"charts.board.frontier.basis\"></div>\n804\t+        </div>\n805\t+\n806\t+        <!-- LAST HIT. Peter, 2026-08-03: \"why is there no last hit card at the\n807\t+             top with the other cards, giving the location, bearing, km and\n808\t+             age?\" The other three say whether we are trying, whether we are\n809\t+             answered, and how far this period — none of them says what we\n810\t+             actually REACHED, which is the most concrete fact we have.\n811\t+             Every value and the basis string are computed server-side; this\n812\t+             renders them. A hit whose target has no position shows an em dash\n813\t+             rather than 0 km, because the node answered and we simply cannot\n814\t+             say how far — the basis then states that. -->\n815\t+        <div class=\"stat bg-base-100 rounded-lg shadow py-3 px-4\">\n816\t+          <div class=\"stat-title text-xs opacity-100 text-base-content/75\">Last hit</div>\n817\t+          <svg viewBox=\"0 0 200 34\" class=\"w-full -mb-1\" x-html=\"obsSpark(charts.board.last_hit.series, charts.board.last_hit.state)\"></svg>\n818\t+          <div class=\"stat-value text-2xl font-mono tabular-nums\"\n819\t+               :class=\"obsVerdictClass(charts.board.last_hit.state)\"\n820\t+               x-text=\"charts.board.last_hit.km == null ? '—' : charts.board.last_hit.km + ' km'\"></div>\n821\t+          <div class=\"stat-desc text-xs whitespace-normal leading-snug opacity-100 text-base-content/80\" x-text=\"charts.board.last_hit.where\"></div>\n822\t+          <div class=\"stat-desc text-xs whitespace-normal leading-snug opacity-100 text-base-content/60\" x-text=\"charts.board.last_hit.basis\"></div>\n823\t+        </div>\n824\t+\n825\t+      </div>\n826\t+    </template>\n827\t+\n828\t     <div class=\"grid grid-cols-1 lg:grid-cols-4 gap-3 shrink-0\">\n829\t       <div class=\"stat bg-base-100 rounded-lg shadow py-3 px-4\">\n830\t-        <div class=\"stat-title text-xs\">Furthest verified reach</div>\n831\t+        <div class=\"stat-title text-xs opacity-100 text-base-content/75\">Furthest verified reach</div>\n832\t         <div class=\"stat-value text-2xl font-display\"\n833\t              :class=\"reach?.record_km ? 'text-primary' : 'text-base-content/25'\"\n834\t              x-text=\"reach?.record_km ? '≥ ' + reach.record_km + ' km' : '—'\"></div>\n835\t         <!-- \"at least\", never \"the maximum\": silence is censored data (spec §4). -->\n836\t-        <div class=\"stat-desc text-xs\"\n837\t+        <div class=\"stat-desc text-xs opacity-100 text-base-content/80\"\n838\t              x-text=\"reach ? reach.verified + ' of ' + reach.targets + ' targets verified' : 'awaiting the reach model'\"></div>\n839\t         <!-- THE BOARD MUST STATE THE AGE OF ITS EVIDENCE. Every panel here is\n840\t              derived from traceroute_history; presenting five-day-old figures as\n841\t              current is the failure Peter named. Tinted when stale. -->\n842\t-        <div class=\"stat-desc text-xs\" x-show=\"obsFreshness()\"\n843\t+        <div class=\"stat-desc text-xs opacity-100 text-base-content/80\" x-show=\"obsFreshness()\"\n844\t              :class=\"obsIsStale() ? 'text-warning' : 'text-base-content/40'\"\n845\t              x-text=\"obsFreshness()\"></div>\n846\t       </div>\n847\t       <div class=\"stat bg-base-100 rounded-lg shadow py-3 px-4\">\n848\t-        <div class=\"stat-title text-xs\">Doors</div>\n849\t+        <div class=\"stat-title text-xs opacity-100 text-base-content/75\">Doors</div>\n850\t         <div class=\"stat-value text-2xl font-display\"\n851\t              :class=\"relayUsage.length ? 'text-secondary' : 'text-base-content/25'\"\n852\t              x-text=\"relayUsage.length || '—'\"></div>\n853\t-        <div class=\"stat-desc text-xs\">relays carrying our routes</div>\n854\t+        <div class=\"stat-desc text-xs opacity-100 text-base-content/80\">relays carrying our routes</div>\n855\t       </div>\n856\t       <div class=\"stat bg-base-100 rounded-lg shadow py-3 px-4\">\n857\t-        <div class=\"stat-title text-xs\">Bearings recorded</div>\n858\t+        <div class=\"stat-title text-xs opacity-100 text-base-content/75\">Bearings recorded</div>\n859\t         <div class=\"stat-value text-2xl font-display\"\n860\t              :class=\"observations.filter(obsHasAz).length ? 'text-primary' : 'text-base-content/25'\"\n861\t              x-text=\"observations.filter(obsHasAz).length || '—'\"></div>\n862\t-        <div class=\"stat-desc text-xs\">on screen, live</div>\n863\t+        <div class=\"stat-desc text-xs opacity-100 text-base-content/80\">on screen, live</div>\n864\t       </div>\n865\t       <div class=\"stat bg-base-100 rounded-lg shadow py-3 px-4\">\n866\t-        <div class=\"stat-title text-xs\">Nodes seen</div>\n867\t+        <div class=\"stat-title text-xs opacity-100 text-base-content/75\">Nodes seen</div>\n868\t         <div class=\"stat-value text-2xl font-display\"\n869\t              :class=\"obsNodeCount() ? '' : 'text-base-content/25'\"\n870\t              x-text=\"obsNodeCount() || '—'\"></div>\n871\t-        <div class=\"stat-desc text-xs\">on screen, live</div>\n872\t+        <div class=\"stat-desc text-xs opacity-100 text-base-content/80\">on screen, live</div>\n873\t       </div>\n874\t     </div>\n875\t \n876\t@@ -262,13 +536,13 @@\n877\t                       <!-- Tinted by liveness: a node silent for weeks answers\n878\t                            1.8% of the time, one heard today 16-26%. -->\n879\t                       <td class=\"text-right pr-3\"\n880\t-                          :class=\"f.since_sec == null ? 'text-base-content/25' : f.since_sec < 86400 ? 'text-success' : f.since_sec < 604800 ? 'text-base-content/50' : 'text-warning'\"\n881\t+                          :class=\"toneCls(f.since_tone)\"\n882\t                           x-text=\"f.since\"></td>\n883\t                       <td class=\"text-right pr-3 text-base-content/60\" x-text=\"f.hits + '/' + f.attempts\"></td>\n884\t                       <!-- Hit rate is the honest headline for a single target:\n885\t                            189 km on one hit in 63 tries is a rare event, not a link. -->\n886\t                       <td class=\"text-right\"\n887\t-                          :class=\"(f.hits / f.attempts) < 0.1 ? 'text-warning' : 'text-base-content/60'\"\n888\t+                          :class=\"f.hit_rate_tone === 'warning' ? A gateway radio never receives its own transmission, so\n162\t// without this rebroadcast only the sending session (optimistic entry) ever\n163\t// saw a TX message (task message-tx-broadcast).\n164\tlet _broadcastMessageHistory = () => {};\n165\texport function broadcastMessageHistory() { _broadcastMessageHistory(); }\n166\t\n167\t// Assigned inside attachWsRelay; device-remove uses it to drop a removed\n168\t// device from the in-memory state and push a fresh device_list. Before this\n169\t// existed, lastDeviceState was add-only and removed devices were re-broadcast\n170\t// forever (task device-remove-op).\n171\tlet _pruneDevice = () => {};\n172\texport function pruneDevice(mac, nodeId = null) {\n173\t  _liveNodeIds.delete(mac.toUpperCase());\n174\t  _pruneDevice(mac, nodeId);\n175\t}\n176\t\n177\t// Settings live on the WS (settings-via-ws): replayed on connect, re-broadcast\n178\t// after every config write so all tabs see filter/radar changes immediately.\n179\tlet _broadcastSettings = () => {};\n180\t// GENERIC settings-changed hook. A plugin whose feed depends on a config value\n181\t// cannot wait for its own recompute timer — the observatory's is 15 minutes, and a\n182\t// control whose effect appears a quarter of an hour later is not a control. Core\n183\t// must not import or name the plugin (this file once named pac-host seven times),\n184\t// so the plugin subscribes and core merely announces. Task `observatory-window`.\n185\tconst _settingsListeners = [];\n186\texport function registerSettingsListener(fn) { _settingsListeners.push(fn); }\n187\t\n188\texport function broadcastSettings() {\n189\t  _broadcastSettings();\n190\t  for (const fn of _settingsListeners) {\n191\t    // Isolated: one plugin throwing must not stop the others being told, nor\n192\t    // fail the config write that triggered this.\n193\t    try { fn(); } catch (e) { console.error(`[ws-relay] settings listener failed: ${e.message}`); }\n194\t  }\n195\t}\n196\t\n197\t// ─── Plugin hooks ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n198\t//\n199\t// This module is CORE — it serves the WebSocket for every page. It must not\n200\t// import, name or branch on a plugin. It used to do all three for pac-host,\n201\t// seven times, and deleting the alarm therefore meant editing core.\n202\t//\n203\t// Peter, 2026-07-29, on being told six of the seven pre-dated that day's work:\n204\t// \"makes no difference if they were added today or not they break the rules and\n205\t// need fixing.\"\n206\t//\n207\t// A plugin needs exactly three things from here, all generic: broadcast to every\n208\t// client, contribute messages to each new connection, and hint node_status for a\n209\t// num. Core learns nothing about what a plugin is for.\n210\tconst _wsWirings     = [];\n211\tconst _connectReplays = [];\n212\t\n213\t/** Wire a plugin's own event sources to the WS. Called ONCE from\n214\t *  attachWsRelay with the host services — deferred because `broadcast` is a\n215\t *  closure that does not exist at import time. */\n216\texport function registerWsWiring(fn) { _wsWirings.push(fn); }\n217\t\n218\t/** Contribute messages replayed to every NEW connection. Returns an array.\n219\t *  An empty array contributes nothing, which makes \"no plugin installed\" and\n220\t *  \"this plugin has nothing to say yet\" the same code path. */\n221\texport function registerConnectReplay(fn) { _connectReplays.push(fn); }\n222\t\n223\t// Seed from persisted mapping so ownDeviceNums() is correct immediately on cold start.\n224\tfor (const [mac, nodeId] of loadNodeMacMap()) {\n225\t  _liveNodeIds.set(mac, nodeId);\n226\t}\n227\t\n228\t// Resolve a stored message's (device MAC, channel INDEX) to the LOGICAL channel\n229\t// name. The stored `channel` is the per-gateway index in both directions, and the\n230\t// same number is a different channel on different radios ((OMNI,1)=mqtt vs\n231\t// (YAGI,1)=Private) — so resolution MUST be per-device. Pure read of the\n232\t// already-maintained lastDeviceChannels cache; no network call. Unnamed PRIMARY\n233\t// resolves to 'Primary' (its identity by role); unknown/uncached → null.\n234\texport function getChannelNameByMac(mac, index) {\n235\t  if (!mac) return null;\n236\t  const chans = lastDeviceChannels[mac.toUpperCase()];\n237\t  if (!Array.isArray(chans)) return null;\n238\t  const c = chans.find(c => c.index === Number(index));\n239\t  if (!c) return null;\n240\t  return c.name || (c.role === 'PRIMARY' ? 'Primary' : null);\n241\t}\n242\t\n243\texport function getLiveNodeIdByMac(mac) {\n244\t  return mac ? (_liveNodeIds.get(mac.toUpperCase()) ?? null) : null;\n245\t}\n246\t\n247\texport function getLiveMacByNodeId(nodeId) {\n248\t  if (!nodeId) return null;\n249\t  for (const [mac, id] of _liveNodeIds.entries()) {"}]},"uuid":"5723937e-faf4-447c-ab7c-b5892db9b261","timestamp":"2026-08-04T10:25:47.274Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"acc2f3cf-8cbf-4cd6-a95d-396053d2216a","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/usr/share/pac/dev/projects/mt-radar/node-dash","sessionId":"ce1ab9c9-df2f-4f79-a39d-d17da291a371","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"feat/alarm-transport-plugin","slug":"elegant-swimming-wirth"}

{"parentUuid":"fcfab577-41f2-4d19-8e50-5ffa6fc34e24","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"4cc8afad-ad11-43a6-b5c0-6e6b0bd41ce6","agentId":"a2755cdc18585e64b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01XxucfCMfop2YWJTy5gzYnJ","type":"tool_result","content":"1510\t// `ts >= null` would drop every row and render an empty page.\n1511\tfunction _failureEpoch() {\n1512\t  const v = getConfig('perf.failure_epoch', null);\n1513\t  const n = Number(v);\n1514\t  return Number.isFinite(n) && n > 0 ? n : null;\n1515\t}\n1516\t\n1517\tfunction _epochSql(col = 'ts') {\n1518\t  const e = _failureEpoch();\n1519\t  return e ? `AND ${col} >= ${e}` : '';\n1520\t}\n1521\t\n1522\texport function attemptsByDay(days = 30) {\n1523\t  return db.prepare(`\n1524\t    SELECT date(ts, 'unixepoch')                            AS day,\n1525\t           COUNT(*)                                         AS attempts,\n1526\t           SUM(CASE WHEN status = 'ok' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)   AS answers\n1527\t    FROM traceroute_history\n1528\t    WHERE 1=1 ${_windowSql(days, 'ts')}\n1529\t    GROUP BY day ORDER BY day\n1530\t  `).all();\n1531\t}\n1532\t\n1533\t/** Answer rate against how long the target had been silent. The strongest\n1534\t *  predictor measured (26.1 / 16.0 / 7.0 / 1.8 %) and the justification for\n1535\t *  max_silence_days — on screen so the setting can be argued with. */\n1536\texport function answerRateBySilence(days = 0) {\n1537\t  return db.prepare(`\n1538\t    WITH heard AS (SELECT DISTINCT num FROM signal_history)\n1539\t    SELECT CASE\n1540\t             WHEN h.num IS NOT NULL                                        THEN 'direct RF'\n1541\t             WHEN n.last_heard > strftime('%s','now') - 86400              THEN '< 24h'\n1542\t             WHEN n.last_heard > strftime('%s','now') - 604800             THEN '< 7d'\n1543\t             ELSE 'silent 7d+'\n1544\t           END                                              AS bucket,\n1545\t           COUNT(*)                                         AS attempts,\n1546\t           SUM(CASE WHEN th.status = 'ok' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS answers\n1547\t    FROM traceroute_history th\n1548\t    LEFT JOIN nodes n ON n.num = th.to_num\n1549\t    LEFT JOIN heard h ON h.num = th.to_num\n1550\t    WHERE 1=1 ${_windowSql(days, 'th.ts')} ${_epochSql('th.ts')}\n1551\t    GROUP BY bucket\n1552\t  `).all();\n1553\t}\n1554\t\n1555\t/** Answer rate by 25 km band. The chart that settles whether a run of silence is\n1556\t *  the targets or the setup. Distance is computed in SQL from the stored\n1557\t *  positions and the configured home. */\n1558\texport function answerRateByDistance(days = 0) {\n1559\t  return db.prepare(`\n1560\t    WITH home AS (\n1561\t      SELECT (SELECT CAST(value AS REAL) FROM config WHERE key='home.lat') AS lat,\n1562\t             (SELECT CAST(value AS REAL) FROM config WHERE key='home.lon') AS lon\n1563\t    ),\n1564\t    d AS (\n1565\t      SELECT th.status,\n1566\t             6371.0 * 2 * asin(min(1.0, sqrt(\n1567\t               power(sin((n.lat - home.lat) * 0.0174532925 / 2), 2) +\n1568\t               cos(home.lat * 0.0174532925) * cos(n.lat * 0.0174532925) *\n1569\t               power(sin((n.lon - home.lon) * 0.0174532925 / 2), 2)\n1570\t             ))) AS km\n1571\t      FROM traceroute_history th\n1572\t      JOIN nodes n ON n.num = th.to_num\n1573\t      CROSS JOIN home\n1574\t      WHERE n.lat IS NOT NULL AND n.lat <> 0 AND home.lat IS NOT NULL\n1575\t        ${_windowSql(days, 'th.ts')} ${_epochSql('th.ts')}\n1576\t    )\n1577\t    SELECT CAST(km / 25 AS INTEGER) * 25                    AS band,\n1578\t           COUNT(*)                                         AS attempts,\n1579\t           SUM(CASE WHEN status = 'ok' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)   AS answers\n1580\t    FROM d WHERE km <= 250\n1581\t    GROUP BY band ORDER BY band\n1582\t  `).all();\n1583\t}\n1584\t\n1585\t/** Answer rate per day SPLIT BY DISTANCE BAND. Task `rate-by-band-by-day`.\n1586\t *\n1587\t *  The aggregate daily series cannot separate a change in TARGET MIX from a\n1588\t *  change in PERFORMANCE, and that ambiguity hid a three-week collapse: the\n1589\t *  answer rate to nodes within 25 km fell from ~58% (early July) to ~7% (late\n1590\t *  July), while the 100+ km band sat at 0-4% throughout and never moved. Close\n1591\t *  targets cannot be explained by mix, so that is the rig degrading — and it\n1592\t *  reorders the project, because pursuing 200 km while 20 km is broken is the\n1593\t *  wrong problem.\n1594\t *\n1595\t *  LEFT JOIN, not the INNER JOIN answerRateByDistance() uses: position-less\n1596\t *  targets are a THIRD of today's attempts and answer at 15-21%. Dropping them\n1597\t *  would hide where a third of the airtime goes. They get their own bucket\n1598\t *  because they have no distance — never folded into a band.\n1599\t *\n1600\t *  `rate` is NULL below MIN_ATTEMPTS. 2026-08-02 shows 24% on n=21 and that is\n1601\t *  noise; a page drawing it as a recovery would mislead. The threshold lives\n1602\t *  here and not in the chart because BROWSER_CONTRACT gives the browser zero\n1603\t *  decisions — it renders a gap where rate is null and never qualifies a number\n1604\t *  itself. attempts/answers are always returned so the gap is still auditable.\n1605\t */\n1606\tconst BAND_MIN_ATTEMPTS = 15;\n1607\t\n1608\texport function rateByBandByDay(days = 30) {\n1609\t  const rows = db.prepare(`\n1610\t    WITH home AS (\n1611\t      SELECT (SELECT CAST(value AS REAL) FROM config WHERE key='home.lat') AS lat,\n1612\t             (SELECT CAST(value AS REAL) FROM config WHERE key='home.lon') AS lon\n1613\t    ),\n1614\t    d AS (\n1615\t      SELECT date(th.ts, 'unixepoch') AS day,\n1616\t             th.status                AS status,\n1617\t             CASE WHEN n.lat IS NULL OR n.lat = 0 THEN NULL ELSE\n1618\t               6371.0 * 2 * asin(min(1.0, sqrt(\n1619\t                 power(sin((n.lat - home.lat) * 0.0174532925 / 2), 2) +\n1620\t                 cos(home.lat * 0.0174532925) * cos(n.lat * 0.0174532925) *\n1621\t                 power(sin((n.lon - home.lon) * 0.0174532925 / 2), 2)\n1622\t               )))\n1623\t             END AS km\n1624\t      FROM traceroute_history th\n1625\t      LEFT JOIN nodes n ON n.num = th.to_num\n1626\t      CROSS JOIN home\n1627\t      WHERE 1=1 ${_windowSql(days, 'th.ts')}\n1628\t    )\n1629\t    SELECT day,\n1630\t           CASE WHEN km IS NULL  THEN 'no-pos'\n1631\t                WHEN km <  25    THEN '<25'\n1632\t                WHEN km < 100    THEN '25-100'\n1633\t                ELSE                  '100+'\n1634\t           END                                              AS band,\n1635\t           COUNT(*)                                         AS attempts,\n1636\t           SUM(CASE WHEN status = 'ok' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)   AS answers\n1637\t    FROM d\n1638\t    GROUP BY day, band\n1639\t    ORDER BY day, band\n1640\t  `).all();\n1641\t  // A day is CENSORED if any part of it precedes perf.failure_epoch: before that\n1642\t  // moment only successes were stored, so the day computes as 100%. 2026-07-03\n1643\t  // straddles the boundary and is treated as censored — a partly-censored day\n1644\t  // cannot honestly be presented as a rate.\n1645\t  //\n1646\t  // The rate is NULLED rather than the row dropped, so `attempts` and `answers`\n1647\t  // stay visible and a reader can see WHY the rate is absent instead of finding a\n1648\t  // silent hole. The chart already breaks its line on a null rate.\n1649\t  const epoch = _failureEpoch();\n1650\t  const censoredBefore = epoch\n1651\t    ? new Date(epoch * 1000).toISOString().slice(0, 10)\n1652\t    : null;\n1653\t  return rows.map(r => ({\n1654\t    ...r,\n1655\t    censored: censoredBefore ? r.day <= censoredBefore : false,\n1656\t    rate: (censoredBefore && r.day <= censoredBefore) || r.attempts < BAND_MIN_ATTEMPTS\n1657\t      ? null\n1658\t      : Math.round((100 * r.answers) / r.attempts),\n1659\t  }));\n1660\t}\n1661\t\n1662\t/** Furthest target ANSWERED inside the window. Task `observatory-window`.\n1663\t *\n1664\t *  NOT the record. `reach.ladder`'s record_km is all-time and stays that way —\n1665\t *  it is the thing to beat (189.1 km, 2026-07-04), and windowed to 7 days it\n1666\t *  would read ~92 km and imply we had gone backwards. Both belong on screen, labelled; serving only the\n1669\t *  all-time figure is how the ladder came to imply we were still reaching\n1670\t *  189.1 km while the last twelve hours topped out at 92.\n1671\t *\n1672\t *  Returns null when nothing was answered in the window — an honest empty, not a\n1673\t *  zero, because 0 km would draw as a data point.\n1674\t */\n1675\texport function windowBestKm(days = 0) {\n1676\t  const row = db.prepare(`\n1677\t    WITH home AS (\n1678\t      SELECT (SELECT CAST(value AS REAL) FROM config WHERE key='home.lat') AS lat,\n1679\t             (SELECT CAST(value AS REAL) FROM config WHERE key='home.lon') AS lon\n1680\t    )\n1681\t    SELECT MAX(\n1682\t             6371.0 * 2 * asin(min(1.0, sqrt(\n1683\t               power(sin((n.lat - home.lat) * 0.0174532925 / 2), 2) +\n1684\t               cos(home.lat * 0.0174532925) * cos(n.lat * 0.0174532925) *\n1685\t               power(sin((n.lon - home.lon) * 0.0174532925 / 2), 2)\n1686\t             )))\n1687\t           ) AS km,\n1688\t           COUNT(*) AS answered\n1689\t    FROM traceroute_history th"}]},"uuid":"c8fb95ea-8024-45fe-9bb1-b0d6ff95fb4d","timestamp":"2026-08-04T10:25:48.126Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"fcfab577-41f2-4d19-8e50-5ffa6fc34e24","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/usr/share/pac/dev/projects/mt-radar/node-dash","sessionId":"ce1ab9c9-df2f-4f79-a39d-d17da291a371","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"feat/alarm-transport-plugin","slug":"elegant-swimming-wirth"}

{"parentUuid":"e2ceced7-e988-45b0-833c-d49c4d075acd","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"4cc8afad-ad11-43a6-b5c0-6e6b0bd41ce6","agentId":"a2755cdc18585e64b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01Wn4qwHy1MLYS86xaFC7AFN","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t// hooks — named, self-describing units of work.\n2\t//\n3\t// Task `event-log`. Spec: docs/modules/hooks.md\n4\t//\n5\t// WHY THE CEREMONY EARNS ITS KEEP. Peter, 2026-08-03: \"the beauty of hooks is\n6\t// they can describe what they are doing… otherwise hooks may just as well be\n7\t// small funcs integrated into the core code.\"\n8\t//\n9\t// Exactly so. Registration and naming are overhead UNLESS a hook is obliged to\n10\t// say what it is and report what it did. That obligation is the entire feature,\n11\t// and it is enforced here by throwing rather than by convention.\n12\t//\n13\t// Every failure found on 2026-08-02/03 was a step that ran and said nothing:\n14\t// a mode gate that returned silently for 9.5 hours; an aim that executed\n15\t// perfectly at 148 degrees off; a filter that dropped 191 candidates; a master\n16\t// switch that sat false for five days. None of those is a missing log line —\n17\t// they are steps with no obligation to speak.\n18\t\n19\timport { insertEventLog } from './db.js';\n20\t\n21\t// ONE UNIFORM STRUCTURE. Peter, 2026-08-03: \"shouldn't our commanding of the\n22\t// pointer also be a hook, instead of buried in the core code... in terms of a\n23\t// uniform structure.\"\n24\t//\n25\t//   store      really WORKER — runs on a cadence, mines what we already hold.\n26\t//              Nothing hooks into it.\n27\t//   per-node   announces a decision taken about ONE node.\n28\t//   per-mode   announces a decision gated by the dashboard mode.\n29\t//   actuator   announces an ACT ON THE WORLD — transmitting, or moving the\n30\t//              antenna — emitted at that actuator's OWN single chokepoint so no\n31\t//              caller can act silently and no future caller has to remember to.\n32\t//\n33\t// `actuator` replaced a narrower `transmit` scope added earlier the same day.\n34\t// Two names for \"acts on the world\" is precisely the non-uniformity Peter is\n35\t// pointing at, and it cost nothing to fold: event_log held 0 rows of the old\n36\t// kind because the callout engine ships disabled.\n37\tconst SCOPES = ['store', 'per-node', 'per-mode', 'actuator'];\n38\t\n39\t// ADDRESSABLE BY SCOPE, THEN BY NAME. Peter, 2026-08-03: \"shouldnt hooks be an\n40\t// object: {'per-node':{do this(), do that()}, xxx:{xxx(), yyy()} - effectively\n41\t// addressable\".\n42\t//\n43\t// Right, and it changes how consumers read. A flat list makes every caller\n44\t// filter; this makes the grouping structural:\n45\t//\n46\t//     for (const h of Object.values(hooks['per-node'])) ...   // the probe steps\n47\t//     hooks['per-node']['select-target'].emit({...})          // one, by name\n48\t//\n49\t// Insertion order is preserved for string keys, so REGISTRATION ORDER IS\n50\t// EXECUTION ORDER for a scope's hooks. That is deliberate and load-bearing for\n51\t// `per-node`, whose steps are a sequence — declare them in the order they run.\n52\texport const hooks = { store: {}, 'per-node': {}, 'per-mode': {}, actuator: {} };\n53\t\n54\t// Names stay globally unique even though the object is nested: `source` in\n55\t// event_log is a bare name, and two hooks sharing one would make the log\n56\t// ambiguous exactly where it is read.\n57\tconst _names = new Set();\n58\t\n59\t// LIVE PUSH. Peter wants the board to feel alive; an event that only lands in a\n60\t// table is not alive until something carries it. Subscribers are notified after\n61\t// the row is written, so a listener never sees an event the database does not\n62\t// have. Core announces; the plugin subscribes — same shape as\n63\t// registerSettingsListener, and this file names no consumer.\n64\tconst _listeners = [];\n65\texport function onEvent(fn) { _listeners.push(fn); }\n66\t\n67\tfunction _announce(row) {\n68\t  for (const fn of _listeners) {\n69\t    // Isolated: a dead socket must not break the next listener, nor the work\n70\t    // that emitted.\n71\t    try { fn(row); } catch (e) { console.error(`[hooks] listener failed: ${e.message}`); }\n72\t  }\n73\t}\n74\t\n75\t/** Register a hook. Throws unless it declares what it is.\n76\t *\n77\t *  The throw is the point. \"Add a step that says nothing\" must be IMPOSSIBLE,\n78\t *  not merely discouraged — a convention is exactly what failed all through\n79\t *  2026-08-02/03.\n80\t *\n81\t *  scope decides the trigger and what the hook is handed:\n82\t *    store     a cadence, the whole database   (ghost-relays, asymmetric-paths)\n83\t *    per-node  a probe_id and one target       (select, history, dispatch)\n84\t *    per-mode  only the modes that permit it   (passive tracing, scanner)\n85\t *\n86\t *  `per-mode` is today's headline bug made structural. Passive tracing carried\n87\t *  its rule as a bare `dashMode.value !== 0` inside the consumer; declaring\n88\t *  modes here puts the list somewhere enumerable and stops a hook holding a\n89\t *  hidden mode check. A declared scope can be inspected; an inline conditional\n90\t *  cannot.\n91\t */\n92\texport function defineHook({ name, purpose, scope, modes = null } = {}) {\n93\t  if (!name || typeof name !== 'string')       throw new Error('defineHook: name is required');\n94\t  if (!purpose || typeof purpose !== 'string') throw new Error(`defineHook(${name}): purpose is required — a hook that cannot say what it is for is a function`);\n95\t  if (!SCOPES.includes(scope))                 throw new Error(`defineHook(${name}): scope must be one of ${SCOPES.join(', ')}`);\n96\t  // `modes` may be a FUNCTION, resolved on first read. A per-mode hook usually\n97\t  // derives its list from dash-mode, and calling that during module evaluation\n98\t  // hit a circular-import TDZ error that took the service down on 2026-08-03.\n99\t  // A thunk defers the read to after every module has initialised.\n100\t  if (scope === 'per-mode') {\n101\t    const ok = typeof modes === 'function' || (Array.isArray(modes) && modes.length);\n102\t    if (!ok) throw new Error(`defineHook(${name}): scope 'per-mode' must declare which modes it runs in`);\n103\t  }\n104\t  if (_names.has(name)) throw new Error(`defineHook: ${name} is already registered`);\n105\t\n106\t  const hook = {\n107\t    name, purpose, scope,\n108\t    get modes() { return typeof modes === 'function' ? modes() : modes; },\n109\t    /** Report what happened. `summary` is written HERE, at the decision site —\n110\t     *  never composed downstream, because a trace assembled elsewhere\n111\t     *  reintroduces the hiding this exists to end.\n112\t     *\n113\t     *  REPORTING NOTHING FOUND IS MANDATORY. \"82 route members checked, 0\n114\t     *  unknown\" is evidence; silence is indistinguishable from the hook being\n115\t     *  broken, which is precisely how the passive tracer failed. */\n116\t    emit({ summary, detail = null, severity = 'info', probe_id = null } = {}) {\n117\t      if (!summary) return;                       // nothing to say is a caller bug, not a crash\n118\t      try {\n119\t        const row = {\n120\t          ts:       Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000),\n121\t          // SCOPE IS NOT KIND, and conflating them mis-filed real events.\n122\t          // Peter, 2026-08-03: \"should it be a hook? — not sure and maybe hook\n123\t          // is the wrong term, should be 'workers'?\" He is right, and this line\n124\t          // is the evidence: `store` was doing double duty, meaning both\n125\t          // \"operates on the whole store\" AND \"runs on a cadence\". A public\n126\t          // transmission is neither, so `callout-sent` was being filed as\n127\t          // kind 'task' beside \"58 nodes have never been traced\".\n128\t          kind:     scope === 'per-node' ? 'probe'\n129\t                  : scope === 'per-mode' ? 'mode'\n130\t                  : scope === 'actuator' ? 'action'\n131\t                  : 'task',\n132\t          severity,\n133\t          summary,\n134\t          detail:   detail == null ? null : JSON.stringify(detail),\n135\t          source:   name,\n136\t          probe_id,\n137\t        };\n138\t        insertEventLog(row);\n139\t        _announce(row);\n140\t      } catch (e) {\n141\t        // A BROKEN LOG MUST NOT STOP THE WORK IT DESCRIBES. Emitting runs inside\n142\t        // the discovery loop; a full disk or a locked database must cost us a\n143\t        // log line, never a probe.\n144\t        console.error(`[hooks] ${name} emit failed: ${e.message}`);\n145\t      }\n146\t    },\n147\t  };\n148\t  _names.add(name);\n149\t  hooks[scope][name] = hook;\n150\t  return hook;\n151\t}\n152\t\n153\t/** THE PIPELINE, ENUMERABLE. Answers \"what does this system execute, and what\n154\t *  does each step claim to do?\" without reading code — the direct answer to\n155\t *  Peter's complaint that the logic is hidden, and something logging alone\n156\t *  cannot provide. */\n157\texport function listHooks() {\n158\t  return SCOPES.flatMap(s => Object.values(hooks[s]))\n159\t    .map(({ name, purpose, scope, modes }) => ({ name, purpose, scope, modes }));\n160\t}\n161\t\n162\texport function getHook(name) {\n163\t  for (const s of SCOPES) if (hooks[s][name]) return hooks[s][name];\n164\t  return null;\n165\t}\n166\t\n167\t/** Direct path for events that are not hook emissions — a config write, a boot.\n168\t *  Same isolation rule: logging must never throw into the caller. */\n169\texport function logEvent({ kind, severity = 'info', summary, detail = null, source = null, probe_id = null } = {}) {\n170\t  if (!kind || !summary) return;\n171\t  try {\n172\t    const row = {\n173\t      ts: Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000),\n174\t      kind, severity, summary,\n175\t      detail: detail == null ? null : JSON.stringify(detail),\n176\t      source, probe_id,\n177\t    };\n178\t    insertEventLog(row);\n179\t    _announce(row);\n180\t  } catch (e) {\n181\t    console.error(`[hooks] logEvent(${kind}) failed: ${e.message}`);\n182\t  }\n183\t}\n184\t\n185\t/** One probe's steps share an id so the trace can be grouped. Time-ordered and\n186\t *  human-legible on purpose — this ends up on screen beside the events it\n187\t *  groups. Date.now() base 36 keeps it short and sortable. */\n188\texport function newProbeId() {\n189\t  return `p${Date.now().toString(36)}${Math.floor(Math.random() * 1296).toString(36).padStart(2, '0')}`;\n190\t}\n191\t"}]},"uuid":"46d5de54-f070-4b8d-ad3f-a67ee9004cce","timestamp":"2026-08-04T10:26:03.579Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"e2ceced7-e988-45b0-833c-d49c4d075acd","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/usr/share/pac/dev/projects/mt-radar/node-dash","sessionId":"ce1ab9c9-df2f-4f79-a39d-d17da291a371","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"feat/alarm-transport-plugin","slug":"elegant-swimming-wirth"}
