{"parentUuid":"e9ee5303-cc50-4b49-8c75-b1053c581b21","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"4cc8afad-ad11-43a6-b5c0-6e6b0bd41ce6","agentId":"a2755cdc18585e64b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01UXp5wbNBTqojuxMGre7WfL","type":"tool_result","content":"// Browser-only utilities shared across mixin modules.\n// haversine/bearing/signalQuality come from /utils.js (classic script) → window globals.\n\nexport const haversine     = (...a) => window.haversine(...a);\nexport const bearing       = (...a) => window.bearing(...a);\nexport const signalQuality = (...a) => window.signalQuality(...a);\nexport const numToNodeId   = (...a) => window.numToNodeId(...a);\nexport const nodeIdToNum   = (...a) => window.nodeIdToNum(...a);\n\n// ── Tones and bands ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n//\n// The ONE thing this layer is allowed to decide: how a semantic tone looks.\n// Mapping `success` to a CSS class is presentation. Deciding that 12 dB is\n// \"Good\" is not, and no longer happens anywhere in public/ — the server serves\n// either a per-row verdict or the band table itself.\n//\n// BROWSER_CONTRACT: \"if a display differs based on a condition, that condition\n// is evaluated in Node.js.\"\nexport const TONE = {\n  success: { cls: 'text-success',         badge: 'badge-success', colour: 'oklch(var(--su))' },\n  info:    { cls: 'text-info',            badge: 'badge-info',    colour: 'oklch(var(--in))' },\n  warning: { cls: 'text-warning',         badge: 'badge-warning', colour: 'oklch(var(--wa))' },\n  error:   { cls: 'text-error',           badge: 'badge-error',   colour: 'oklch(var(--er))' },\n  muted:   { cls: 'text-base-content/30', badge: 'badge-ghost',   colour: 'oklch(var(--bc)/0.15)' },\n};\nexport const toneCls    = t => (TONE[t] ?? TONE.muted).cls;\nexport const toneBadge  = t => (TONE[t] ?? TONE.muted).badge;\nexport const toneColour = t => (TONE[t] ?? TONE.muted).colour;\n\n/** Look a value up in a SERVED band table. The bands and their order come from\n *  the server; this walks them. `min: null` is the catch-all — JSON cannot\n *  carry -Infinity, so the server emits null and it always matches.\n *\n *  Returns null when the table has not arrived yet, so callers render a muted\n *  placeholder rather than inventing a band. */\nexport function bandFor(value, bands) {\n  if (!Array.isArray(bands) || !bands.length) return null;\n  if (value == null || !Number.isFinite(Number(value))) return null;\n  const v = Number(value);\n  return bands.find(b => b.min == null || v >= b.min) ?? bands[bands.length - 1];\n}\n\n/** Same, for a SERVED descending table (`max`, lower-is-better). Kept separate\n *  rather than overloading bandFor: silently applying the ascending rule to a\n *  descending table would mis-colour every row and look like it worked. */\nexport function bandForMax(value, bands) {\n  if (!Array.isArray(bands) || !bands.length) return null;\n  if (value == null || !Number.isFinite(Number(value))) return null;\n  const v = Number(value);\n  return bands.find(b => b.max == null || v < b.max) ?? bands[bands.length - 1];\n}\n\nexport async function fetchJSON(url, method = 'GET', body) {\n  const opts = { method, headers: {} };\n  if (body !== undefined) {\n    opts.headers['Content-Type'] = 'application/json';\n    opts.body = JSON.stringify(body);\n  }\n  const res = await fetch(url, opts);\n  const data = await res.json().catch(() => null);","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"c27ed69e-b10f-4e54-b5ea-8ec6872ec8f3","timestamp":"2026-08-04T10:26:17.174Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"e9ee5303-cc50-4b49-8c75-b1053c581b21","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/usr/share/pac/dev/projects/mt-radar/node-dash","sessionId":"ce1ab9c9-df2f-4f79-a39d-d17da291a371","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"feat/alarm-transport-plugin","slug":"elegant-swimming-wirth"}

NOT CORE.\n2\t//\n3\t// Same shape and the same reason as alarm-ws.js: src/ws-relay.js serves the WS\n4\t// for every page and must not name a plugin. This file knows the observatory;\n5\t// core does not know this file. Delete it and its one import in index.js and\n6\t// core is unchanged.\n7\t//\n8\t// It is the THIRD file permitted to import observatory.js, and that is a\n9\t// deliberate decision rather than a boundary quietly slipping. The rule the\n10\t// test encodes is \"CORE must not reach into the engine\". This is a plugin — the\n11\t// engine's own WS wiring — exactly as alarm-ws.js is the alarm's. The allowlist\n12\t// names it explicitly so a fourth entry is again a visible choice.\n13\t//\n14\t// See docs/WS_PLUGIN_HOOKS_SPEC.md and docs/PLUGIN_BOUNDARY_SPEC.md.\n15\timport { events, recentObservations, facts, runInference } from './observatory.js';\n16\timport { registerWsWiring, registerConnectReplay, registerSettingsListener } from './ws-relay.js';\n17\timport { onEvent, listHooks } from './hooks.js';\n18\timport { resolveNodeLabel } from './node-label.js';\n19\timport { mqttDiscarded } from './observations.js';\n20\timport { missionRunner } from './mission-runner.js';\n21\timport { hitRateVerdict } from './signal-verdict.js';\n22\timport db, { getConfig, getCachedGeocode, recentAimedTraceroutes, lastHeardMap, positionMap, listObsTargets,\n23\t         attemptsByDay, answerRateBySilence, answerRateByDistance,\n24\t         rateByBandByDay, windowBestKm, boardVerdicts, recentEventLog, interventions } from './db.js';\n25\t\n26\t// Read once: the map marks where we are, and the reach model already computes\n27\t// every distance from it. A page load must not re-read config.\n28\tconst HOME = { lat: getConfig('home.lat'), lon: getConfig('home.lon') };\n29\t\n30\t// How much history a freshly-connected page starts with. On a quiet channel a\n31\t// page that waits for the next packet looks broken for minutes, so it opens with\n32\t// the recent past already on screen and grows from there.\n33\tconst REPLAY_LIMIT = 200;\n34\t\n35\t/** One stored row as a WS message.\n36\t *\n37\t *  `data` is re-parsed rather than passed through as a string: the browser must\n38\t *  not be handed JSON-inside-JSON to unpick. Parse failures yield null instead\n39\t *  of throwing — a single malformed payload must not stop the feed.\n40\t *\n41\t *  BROWSER_CONTRACT: this is raw observation data, not display values. Anything\n42\t *  the page needs FORMATTED (relative ages, labels) is a server-computed field\n43\t *  and must be added here, never derived in the browser. */\n44\tfunction toMessage(row) {\n45\t  let data = null;\n46\t  try { data = row.data ? JSON.parse(row.data) : null; } catch { data = null; }\n47\t  return { id: row.id, ts: row.ts, kind: row.kind, entity: row.entity, source: row.source, data };\n48\t}\n49\t\n50\t// Live pushes. Core hands us `broadcast`; we know the observatory, core does not\n51\t// know us.\n52\tlet _broadcast = null;\n53\tregisterWsWiring(({ broadcast }) => {\n54\t  _broadcast = broadcast;\n55\t  // Live, one row at a time, as it is emitted — not on the 15-minute recompute.\n56\t  // This is what makes the page a feed rather than a report.\n57\t  onEvent(row => {\n58\t    try { broadcast({ type: 'event', event: row }); }\n59\t    catch (e) { console.error(`[observatory-ws] event push failed: ${e.message}`); }\n60\t  });"}]},"uuid":"21f9c236-3d52-46e6-9f56-ed578ccbd268","timestamp":"2026-08-04T10:26:39.917Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"615f2946-d74d-4c28-a581-b80164733d18","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/usr/share/pac/dev/projects/mt-radar/node-dash","sessionId":"ce1ab9c9-df2f-4f79-a39d-d17da291a371","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"feat/alarm-transport-plugin","slug":"elegant-swimming-wirth"}

Measured\n773\t// 2026-08-04: this set captures 19 of the 99 nodes that have actually relayed for\n774\t// us, so filtering doors on it discards 80.8% of them. See docs/APP_ROLE.md.\n775\tconst RELAY_ROLE_SET = new Set(['ROUTER', 'ROUTER_LATE', 'ROUTER_CLIENT', 'REPEATER']);\n776\t\n777\t/** Bearing FROM us, degrees. Duplicated from the catalogue for the same reason\n778\t *  haversine is: this layer must not import domain calculations. */\n779\tfunction bearingFromHome(lat, lon) {\n780\t  if (lat == null || lon == null || HOME.lat == null || HOME.lon == null) return null;\n781\t  const r = Math.PI / 180;\n782\t  const y = Math.sin((lon - HOME.lon) * r) * Math.cos(lat * r);\n783\t  const x = Math.cos(HOME.lat * r) * Math.sin(lat * r)\n784\t          - Math.sin(HOME.lat * r) * Math.cos(lat * r) * Math.cos((lon - HOME.lon) * r);\n785\t  return Math.round((Math.atan2(y, x) / r + 360) % 360);\n786\t}\n787\t\n788\t/** Great-circle km. Duplicated from the catalogue on purpose: this file must not\n789\t *  import domain calculations, and an inference must not be imported for its\n790\t *  arithmetic — that coupling is how the engine's boundary starts to leak. */\n791\tfunction haversine(lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2) {\n792\t  const rad = Math.PI / 180;\n793\t  const dLat = (lat2 - lat1) * rad, dLon = (lon2 - lon1) * rad;\n794\t  const a = Math.sin(dLat / 2) ** 2\n795\t    + Math.cos(lat1 * rad) * Math.cos(lat2 * rad) * Math.sin(dLon / 2) ** 2;\n796\t  return 6371 * 2 * Math.asin(Math.min(1, Math.sqrt(a)));\n797\t}\n798\t\n799\t// RECOMPUTED ON BOOT, then on a slow timer. `relay.usage` is a batch inference\n800\t// over all history — cheap at this size (12,268 evidence rows, ~40 ms) but not\n801\t// something to run per packet, and the answer moves slowly: a new relay appears\n802\t// only when a traceroute completes through it.\n803\t//\n804\t// Deferred past startup so a slow query cannot delay the port opening, and\n805\t// wrapped so a failure degrades the doors panel rather than the process.\n806\tfunction recompute(broadcast) {\n807\t  try {\n808\t    for (const key of ['relay.usage', 'reach.target', 'reach.ladder', 'reach.heard', 'link.observed', 'reach.mission']) {\n809\t      const r = runInference(key);\n810\t      console.log(`[observatory] ${r.key}: ${r.facts} facts from ${r.rows} evidence rows`);\n811\t    }\n812\t    // ONLY WHEN NON-ZERO. MQTT arrivals never enter the store (§3a) — they are\n813\t    // dropped at the mapper — but a silent discard is indistinguishable from a\n814\t    // quiet channel. Today this number is zero and the line never prints; the\n815\t    // day a gateway starts bridging, it does.\n816\t    const dropped = mqttDiscarded();\n817\t    if (dropped) console.log(`[observatory] ${dropped} MQTT arrivals discarded since boot — not radio hops`);\n818\t\n819\t    broadcast?.({ type: 'relay_usage', relays: relayUsage() });\n820\t    broadcast?.({ type: 'reach_model', reach: reachModel() });\n821\t    broadcast?.({ type: 'mesh_links', links: meshLinks() });\n822\t    const list = missions();\n823\t    broadcast?.({ type: 'missions', missions: list });\n824\t    broadcast?.({ type: 'charts', charts: charts() });\n825\t\n826\t    // FEED THE ACTUATOR. The runner never fetches — it is handed the shortlist\n827\t    // and works it. This file is already an allowed importer of the engine; the\n828\t    // runner deliberately is not, so the coupling lives here.\n829\t    missionRunner.setQueue(list.missions);\n830\t    // Seed what was already known BEFORE any mission ran, so the first route\n831\t    // does not report the whole existing mesh as newly discovered.\n832\t    // Replay recent attempts so the panel is populated on a cold start. Only\n833\t    // the yagi-dispatched ones: those are the discovery runner's, and a\n834\t    // passive-tracer trace of whatever just arrived is not a mission.\n835\t    try {\n836\t      const rows = recentAimedTraceroutes(25);\n837\t      missionRunner.seedRecent(rows.map(r => ({\n838\t        target: String(r.to_num),\n839\t        label:  resolveNodeLabel(r.to_num) || String(r.to_num),\n840\t        place:  shortPlace(getCachedGeocode(r.to_num)),\n841\t        km: null, step_km: null, az: r.rotator_az,\n842\t        seeded: true,\n843\t        ok: r.status === 'ok',\n844\t        hops: null, hops_back: null, found: [],\n845\t        error: r.status === 'ok' ? null : 'no reply',\n846\t        finished: r.ts,\n847\t      })));\n848\t    } catch (e) {\n849\t      console.error(`[observatory-ws] recent replay failed: ${e.message}`);\n850\t    }\n851\t\n852\t    missionRunner.seed({\n853\t      relays: facts('relay.usage').map(f => f.entity),\n854\t      links:  facts('link.observed').map(f => f.entity),\n855\t    });\n856\t  } catch (e) {\n857\t    console.error(`[observatory] relay.usage failed: ${e.message}`);\n858\t  }\n859\t}\n860\t\n861\tregisterWsWiring(({ broadcast }) => {\n862\t  setTimeout(() => recompute(broadcast), 10_000);\n863\t  // Settable (discovery.recompute_min): selection latency is a timing that\n864\t  // changes what the operator sees, so it needs a bounded knob like the rest.\n865\t  // Read once at wiring — a live re-read would need the interval torn down and\n866\t  // rebuilt, which is not worth it for a value nobody changes mid-session.\n867\t  const recomputeMin = Number((getConfig('discovery', {}) || {}).recompute_min) || 15;\n868\t  setInterval(() => recompute(broadcast), recomputeMin * 60_000);\n869\t\n870\t  // Live activity: every state change, pushed. This is the panel Peter asked\n871\t  // for — \"what it's done, doing right now, and whether a discovery is in\n872\t  // progress\" — so it must not wait on the 15-minute recompute.\n873\t  missionRunner.on('activity', (activity) => {\n874\t    try { broadcast({ type: 'mission_activity', activity }); }\n875\t    catch (e) { console.error(`[observatory-ws] activity broadcast failed: ${e.message}`); }\n876\t  });\n877\t\n878\t  // Started after the first recompute has had a chance to fill the queue.\n879\t  setTimeout(() => missionRunner.start(), 20_000);\n880\t});\n881\t"}]},"uuid":"5c23cf57-fb8e-4afe-b1fc-d523856bf464","timestamp":"2026-08-04T10:26:40.823Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"13a3499b-a3c6-4565-a5cd-1e97bb344235","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/usr/share/pac/dev/projects/mt-radar/node-dash","sessionId":"ce1ab9c9-df2f-4f79-a39d-d17da291a371","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"feat/alarm-transport-plugin","slug":"elegant-swimming-wirth"}

They read history rather than the\n  // wire, so they gain nothing from running often. Task `hooks-standing-tasks`.\n  'standing_tasks.every_min': 60,\n  'radar.log_scale':          true,   // adaptive quantile scale (linear = opt-out)\n  'radar.crosshair':          false,\n  'message_filter.channels':  [],\n  'message_filter.hide_mqtt': false,\n  'packet_sources':           [],\n  'range_test.duration':      10,\n  'perf.failure_epoch':       null,   // read-only stamp: when traceroute failure recording began\n  'traceroute.enabled':       true,   // master switch for AUTOMATIC dispatch; a manual request is never gated\n  // DISCOVERY STRATEGY. One key, because this list governs two things at once:\n  // the generic PUT below rejects anything not in it, and ws-relay's\n  // settingsEvent() iterates it to build the settings WS payload. See docs/DISCOVERY_STRATEGY.md.\n=== 370-450 ===\n      cur.rx = upd.rx;\n    }\n    if (upd.tx !== undefined) {\n      if (!isValidRole(upd.tx, 'tx')) return res.status(400).json({ error: `invalid tx role for ${mode}: ${upd.tx}` });\n      cur.tx = upd.tx;\n    }\n    current[mode] = cur;\n  }\n  setConfig('mode_config', current);\n  res.json(modeConfigAll());\n  pokeDeviceList();   // roles changed → refresh RX/TX badges on device_list\n});\n\nrouter.get('/:key', (req, res) => {\n  const { key } = req.params;\n  if (!(key in DEFAULTS)) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Unknown config key' });\n  res.json({ key, value: getConfig(key, DEFAULTS[key]) });\n});\n\nconst NODE_FILTER_KEYS = new Set(Object.keys(DEFAULTS).filter(k => k.startsWith('node_filters.')));\n\nrouter.put('/:key', (req, res) => {\n  const { key } = req.params;\n  if (!(key in DEFAULTS)) return res.status(404).json({ error: 'Unknown config key' });\n  const { value } = req.body;\n  if (value === undefined) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'value required' });\n  if (key === 'observatory.window_days' && !WINDOW_DAYS.includes(Number(value))) {\n    return res.status(400).json({ error: `window_days must be one of ${WINDOW_DAYS.join(', ')}` });\n  }\n  // WHAT CHANGED, WHEN. Without this every \"did that help?\" is answered from\n  // memory — which is how 2026-08-03 went, and it cost hours. The window\n  // selector is excluded: it changes the VIEW, not the system, and is written\n  // constantly. Task `event-log`.\n  const before = getConfig(key, DEFAULTS[key]);\n  setConfig(key, value);\n  if (key !== 'observatory.window_days') {\n    logEvent({\n      kind: 'config', source: 'config-api',\n      severity: key === 'traceroute.enabled' && value === false ? 'notice' : 'info',\n      summary: `${key}: ${JSON.stringify(before)} → ${JSON.stringify(value)}`,\n      detail: { key, from: before, to: value },\n    });\n  }\n  if (NODE_FILTER_KEYS.has(key)) nodeList.refilter();\n  res.json({ key, value });\n  broadcastSettings();\n});\n\nrouter.put('/', (req, res) => {\n  const updates = req.body;\n  if (typeof updates !== 'object' || Array.isArray(updates)) {\n    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'body must be an object of key/value pairs' });\n  }\n  const unknown = Object.keys(updates).filter(k => !(k in DEFAULTS));\n  if (unknown.length) return res.status(400).json({ error: `Unknown keys: ${unknown.join(', ')}` });\n  if ('observatory.window_days' in updates &&\n      !WINDOW_DAYS.includes(Number(updates['observatory.window_days']))) {\n    return res.status(400).json({ error: `window_days must be one of ${WINDOW_DAYS.join(', ')}` });\n  }\n  for (const [key, value] of Object.entries(updates)) {\n    const before = getConfig(key, DEFAULTS[key]);\n    setConfig(key, value);\n    if (key !== 'observatory.window_days') {\n      logEvent({\n        kind: 'config', source: 'config-api',\n        summary: `${key}: ${JSON.stringify(before)} → ${JSON.stringify(value)}`,\n        detail: { key, from: before, to: value },\n      });\n    }\n  }\n  if (Object.keys(updates).some(k => NODE_FILTER_KEYS.has(k))) nodeList.refilter();\n  res.json(updates);\n  broadcastSettings();\n});\n\nexport default router;","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"a435b372-f331-4619-a0cf-36786125af6e","timestamp":"2026-08-04T10:27:20.364Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"c1965f40-52db-4158-8f70-b9ed918f7c1f","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/usr/share/pac/dev/projects/mt-radar/node-dash","sessionId":"ce1ab9c9-df2f-4f79-a39d-d17da291a371","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"feat/alarm-transport-plugin","slug":"elegant-swimming-wirth"}

{"parentUuid":"02afe5ae-7c29-481c-897d-a0582aedd9c6","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"4cc8afad-ad11-43a6-b5c0-6e6b0bd41ce6","agentId":"a2755cdc18585e64b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01FRb3dwNGnwjezSFA1ryEjx","type":"tool_result","content":"1210\texport function boardVerdicts(days = 7) {\n1211\t  const now  = Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000);\n1212\t  const win  = Number(days) > 0 ? Number(days) : 3650;   // \"all time\" still needs a span\n1213\t  const from = now - win * 86400;\n1214\t  const prevFrom = from - win * 86400;\n1215\t  const home = db.prepare(`\n1216\t    SELECT (SELECT CAST(value AS REAL) FROM config WHERE key='home.lat') lat,\n1217\t           (SELECT CAST(value AS REAL) FROM config WHERE key='home.lon') lon`).get();\n1218\t  const KM = `6371.0*2*asin(min(1.0,sqrt(\n1219\t      power(sin((n.lat-@hlat)*0.0174532925/2),2) +\n1220\t      cos(@hlat*0.0174532925)*cos(n.lat*0.0174532925)*\n1221\t      power(sin((n.lon-@hlon)*0.0174532925/2),2))))`;\n1222\t\n1223\t  // ── LOOP ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n1224\t  // Judged against the CONFIGURED cadence, not a fixed number, so the verdict\n1225\t  // follows discovery.interval_sec instead of contradicting it.\n1226\t  const interval = Number((getConfig('discovery', {}) || {}).interval_sec) || 180;\n1227\t  const lastTs = db.prepare('SELECT MAX(ts) t FROM traceroute_history').get()?.t ?? null;\n1228\t  const since  = lastTs ? now - lastTs : null;\n1229\t  const perHour = db.prepare(\n1230\t    `SELECT COUNT(*) n FROM traceroute_history WHERE ts > ?`).get(now - 3600).n;\n1231\t  let loopState = 'fault', loopBasis;\n1232\t  if (since == null) {\n1233\t    loopBasis = 'no attempt has ever been recorded';\n1234\t  } else if (since <= interval * 3) {\n1235\t    loopState = 'ok';   loopBasis = `last attempt ${since}s ago, ${perHour}/hour`;\n1236\t  } else if (since <= interval * 20) {\n1237\t    loopState = 'warn'; loopBasis = `last attempt ${Math.round(since / 60)} min ago, expected every ${interval}s`;\n1238\t  } else {\n1239\t    loopBasis = `nothing sent for ${Math.round(since / 3600)}h — expected every ${interval}s`;\n1240\t  }\n1241\t\n1242\t  // ── YIELD ─────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n1243\t  // Against the PREVIOUS window of equal length. A rate alone cannot show\n1244\t  // degradation; only a comparison can, and degradation is what went unseen for\n1245\t  // three weeks.\n1246\t  const rate = (a, b) => {\n1247\t    const r = db.prepare(\n1248\t      `SELECT COUNT(*) att, SUM(CASE WHEN status='ok' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) ok\n1249\t         FROM traceroute_history WHERE ts > ? AND ts <= ?`).get(a, b);\n1250\t    return { att: r.att, ok: r.ok || 0, pct: r.att ? (100 * (r.ok || 0)) / r.att : null };\n1251\t  };\n1252\t  const cur = rate(from, now), prev = rate(prevFrom, from);\n1253\t  let yState = 'ok', yBasis;\n1254\t  if (!cur.att) {\n1255\t    yState = 'fault'; yBasis = 'no attempts in this window';\n1256\t  } else if (cur.ok === 0 && cur.att >= 20) {\n1257\t    yState = 'fault'; yBasis = `0 of ${cur.att} answered`;\n1258\t  } else if (prev.pct != null && prev.pct > 0) {\n1259\t    const ratio = cur.pct / prev.pct;\n1260\t    yState = ratio >= 0.8 ? 'ok' : ratio >= 0.5 ? 'warn' : 'fault';\n1261\t    yBasis = `${cur.pct.toFixed(0)}% of ${cur.att}, previous period ${prev.pct.toFixed(0)}%`;\n1262\t  } else {\n1263\t    yBasis = `${cur.pct.toFixed(0)}% of ${cur.att} answered`;\n1264\t  }\n1265\t\n1266\t  // ── FRONTIER ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n1267\t  const best = (a, b) => db.prepare(`\n1268\t    SELECT MAX(${KM}) km FROM traceroute_history t JOIN nodes n ON n.num = t.to_num\n1269\t     WHERE t.status='ok' AND n.lat IS NOT NULL AND n.lat <> 0 AND t.ts > @a AND t.ts <= @b`)\n1270\t    .get({ a, b, hlat: home?.lat ?? 0, hlon: home?.lon ?? 0 })?.km ?? null;\n1271\t  const curKm = best(from, now), prevKm = best(prevFrom, from);\n1272\t  const rec = db.prepare(`\n1273\t    SELECT MAX(${KM}) km, MAX(t.ts) ts FROM traceroute_history t JOIN nodes n ON n.num = t.to_num\n1274\t     WHERE t.status='ok' AND n.lat IS NOT NULL AND n.lat <> 0`)\n1275\t    .get({ hlat: home?.lat ?? 0, hlon: home?.lon ?? 0 });\n1276\t  const recordKm = rec?.km != null ? Math.round(rec.km * 10) / 10 : null;\n1277\t  // Days since the ALL-TIME record was last improved — the honest \"are we\n1278\t  // progressing\" number, and the one the board has been hiding behind.\n1279\t  const recAt = recordKm == null ? null : db.prepare(`\n1280\t    SELECT MIN(t.ts) ts FROM traceroute_history t JOIN nodes n ON n.num = t.to_num\n1281\t     WHERE t.status='ok' AND n.lat IS NOT NULL AND ${KM} >= @rk`)\n1282\t    .get({ hlat: home?.lat ?? 0, hlon: home?.lon ?? 0, rk: recordKm - 0.05 })?.ts ?? null;\n1283\t  const flatDays = recAt ? Math.floor((now - recAt) / 86400) : null;\n1284\t  // THE VERDICT ONLY. Each of these used to open with `curKm`, which the card\n1285\t  // prints directly above as its value — so every frontier card said its own\n1286\t  // number twice and had less room for the thing that was actually missing.\n1287\t  let fState = 'warn', fBasis;\n1288\t  if (curKm == null) {\n1289\t    fState = 'fault'; fBasis = 'nothing answered in this window';\n1290\t  } else if (prevKm != null && curKm > prevKm) {\n1291\t    fState = 'ok';    fBasis = `up from ${prevKm.toFixed(1)} km last period`;\n1292\t  } else if (flatDays != null && flatDays > 7) {\n1293\t    fState = 'fault'; fBasis = `record ${recordKm} km unbeaten ${flatDays} days`;\n1294\t  } else {\n1295\t    fBasis = prevKm != null ? `previous period ${prevKm.toFixed(1)} km` : 'no previous period to compare';\n1296\t  }\n1297\t\n1298\t  // ── SPARKLINES ────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n1299\t  // Per day over the window. A day with too few attempts returns null and BREAKS\n1300\t  // the line — the rule already proven in rateByBandByDay. A gap reads as \"not\n1301\t  // known\"; a zero reads as \"nothing happened\", and they are different claims.\n1302\t  const daily = db.prepare(`\n1303\t    SELECT date(t.ts,'unixepoch') day, COUNT(*) att,\n1304\t           SUM(CASE WHEN t.status='ok' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) ok,\n1305\t           MAX(CASE WHEN t.status='ok' THEN ${KM} END) km\n1306\t      FROM traceroute_history t LEFT JOIN nodes n ON n.num = t.to_num\n1307\t     WHERE t.ts > @from GROUP BY day ORDER BY day`)\n1308\t    .all({ from, hlat: home?.lat ?? 0, hlon: home?.lon ?? 0 });\n1309\t  const MIN_ATT = 15;\n1310\t\n1311\t  // THE PLACE NAME, NOT THE POSTAL ADDRESS. Nominatim returns\n1312\t  // \"Clos des Mielles, Castel, GY5 7FX, Guernsey\" and a stat card has room for\n1313\t  // one word of it. The second field is the settlement; the first is the street,\n1314\t  // which locates nothing at 176 km. Falls back to the first field when there\n1315\t  // is only one.\n1316\t  const locality = (addr) => {\n1317\t    if (!addr) return null;\n1318\t    const parts = String(addr).split(',').map(s => s.trim()).filter(Boolean);\n1319\t    return parts[1] || parts[0] || null;\n1320\t  };\n1321\t\n1322\t  // ── LAST HIT ──────────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n1323\t  // Peter, 2026-08-03: \"why is there no last hit card at the top with the other\n1324\t  // cards, giving the location, bearing, km and age?\"\n1325\t  //\n1326\t  // There was no reason. LOOP says whether we are trying, YIELD whether we are\n1327\t  // answered, FRONTIER how far this period — and none of them says WHAT WE\n1328\t  // ACTUALLY REACHED. It is the most concrete fact the system produces, and\n1329\t  // answering \"was that a hit?\" meant a hand query against traceroute_history.\n1330\t  //\n1331\t  // Distance and bearing are computed HERE, not in the browser, along with a\n1332\t  // finished basis string — the browser makes no decisions (BROWSER_CONTRACT).\n1333\t  const lh = db.prepare(`\n1334\t    SELECT t.ts, t.to_num, t.rtt_ms, n.long_name, n.lat, n.lon, g.address\n1335\t      FROM traceroute_history t\n1336\t      LEFT JOIN nodes n         ON n.num = t.to_num\n1337\t      LEFT JOIN geocode_cache g ON g.num = t.to_num\n1338\t     WHERE t.status = 'ok'\n1339\t     ORDER BY t.ts DESC LIMIT 1`).get();\n1340\t\n1341\t  let lastHit;\n1342\t  if (!lh) {\n1343\t    lastHit = { state: 'fault', name: null, km: null, bearing: null, age_sec: null,\n1344\t                place: null, basis: 'nothing has ever answered' };\n1345\t  } else {\n1346\t    const age    = now - Number(lh.ts);\n1347\t    const placed = lh.lat != null && lh.lat !== 0 && lh.lon != null;\n1348\t    // A HIT WE CANNOT PLACE IS STILL A HIT. km and bearing stay NULL rather\n1349\t    // than 0 — the target answered, we simply cannot say how far. Rendering a\n1350\t    // zero here would claim a measurement we never made. Recurring, not\n1351\t    // hypothetical: the only mission answer of 2026-08-03 was Pokey Hollow, a\n1352\t    // HELTEC_V3 with no GPS that has never sent a position.\n1353\t    const km  = placed ? Math.round(haversine(home?.lat, home?.lon, lh.lat, lh.lon) * 10) / 10 : null;\n1354\t    const brg = placed ? Math.round(bearingDeg(home?.lat, home?.lon, lh.lat, lh.lon)) : null;\n1355\t    const name = lh.long_name || `!${Number(lh.to_num).toString(16)}`;\n1356\t    const when = age < 90 ? `${age}s ago`\n1357\t               : age < 5400 ? `${Math.round(age / 60)} min ago`\n1358\t               : age < 172800 ? `${Math.round(age / 3600)}h ago`\n1359\t               : `${Math.round(age / 86400)}d ago`;\n1360\t    // Freshness is judged against the same cadence the loop uses, so the two\n1361\t    // cards cannot disagree about what \"recent\" means.\n1362\t    const state = age <= interval * 20 ? 'ok' : age <= 86400 ? 'warn' : 'fault';\n1363\t    // TWO LINES, ONE JOB EACH. `where` locates it, `basis` times it. Packing\n1364\t    // both into one string produced a 110-character run that overflowed the\n1365\t    // card and repeated the value already printed above it.\n1366\t    lastHit = {\n1367\t      state, name, km, bearing: brg, age_sec: age,\n1368\t      place: lh.address || null,\n1369\t      rtt_ms: lh.rtt_ms == null ? null : Number(lh.rtt_ms),\n1370\t      where: `${name}${placed ? ` · ${brg}°` : ''}`\n1371\t        + (locality(lh.address) ? ` · ${locality(lh.address)}` : ''),\n1372\t      basis: placed ? when : `${when} · position unknown, distance unmeasurable`,\n1373\t    };\n1374\t  }\n1375\t\n1376\t  // ── WHERE THE FRONTIER ACTUALLY IS ────────────────────────────────────────\n1377\t  // Peter, 2026-08-03: \"we need a last hit and a last frontier hit — or maybe\n1378\t  // the frontier card already there needs the location?\"\n1379\t  //\n1380\t  // The second reading is the right one, and the data settles it: the furthest\n1381\t  // answered target IS the number the frontier card already shows. A separate\n1382\t  // card would print 176.2 km twice and add a fifth column for nothing.\n1383\t  //\n1384\t  // So this does not become a card. It gives the existing frontier card the\n1385\t  // three facts it was missing — WHO, WHICH WAY, and WHEN — because \"176.2 km\"\n1386\t  // alone cannot be acted on: you cannot re-point an antenna at a scalar.\n1387\t  //\n1388\t  // LAST HIT stays separate and is genuinely a different question: on this day\n1389\t  // the last hit was 67.5 km 24 minutes earlier while the frontier had not\n1390\t  // moved in 29 days. A board showing only one of those reads as healthy while\n1391\t  // the actual goal is static.\n1392\t  const fh = db.prepare(`\n1393\t    SELECT t.ts, t.to_num, n.long_name, n.lat, n.lon, g.address, ${KM} AS km\n1394\t      FROM traceroute_history t\n1395\t      JOIN nodes n              ON n.num = t.to_num\n1396\t      LEFT JOIN geocode_cache g ON g.num = t.to_num\n1397\t     WHERE t.status = 'ok' AND t.ts > @from\n1398\t       AND n.lat IS NOT NULL AND n.lat <> 0\n1399\t     ORDER BY km DESC, t.ts DESC LIMIT 1`)\n1400\t    .get({ from, hlat: home?.lat ?? 0, hlon: home?.lon ?? 0 });\n1401\t\n1402\t  let frontierHit;\n1403\t  if (!fh) {\n1404\t    frontierHit = { state: 'fault', name: null, km: null, bearing: null, age_sec: null,\n1405\t                    place: null, basis: 'nothing answered in this window' };\n1406\t  } else {\n1407\t    const age = now - Number(fh.ts);\n1408\t    const km  = Math.round(Number(fh.km) * 10) / 10;\n1409\t    const brg = Math.round(bearingDeg(home?.lat, home?.lon, fh.lat, fh.lon));\n1410\t    const name = fh.long_name || `!${Number(fh.to_num).toString(16)}`;\n1411\t    const when = age < 5400 ? `${Math.round(age / 60)} min ago`\n1412\t               : age < 172800 ? `${Math.round(age / 3600)}h ago`\n1413\t               : `${Math.round(age / 86400)}d ago`;\n1414\t    // Judged on AGE, not distance: a frontier reached once and never revisited\n1415\t    // is the stagnation the board exists to expose.\n1416\t    const state = age <= 86400 ? 'ok' : age <= 7 * 86400 ? 'warn' : 'fault';\n1417\t    frontierHit = {\n1418\t      state, name, km, bearing: brg, age_sec: age, place: fh.address || null,\n1419\t      basis: `${name} · ${when} · ${brg}°`\n1420\t        + (fh.address ? ` · ${(String(fh.address).split(',')[1] || String(fh.address).split(',')[0] || '').trim()}` : ''),\n1421\t    };\n1422\t  }\n1423\t\n1424\t  // The frontier card states WHERE it got to, not just how far. Appended rather\n1425\t  // than replacing fBasis, so the verdict (rising / flat / stalled) still leads\n1426\t  // and the location qualifies it.\n1427\t  const fWhere = frontierHit.name\n1428\t    ? `${frontierHit.name} · ${frontierHit.bearing}°`\n1429\t      + (locality(frontierHit.place) ? ` · ${locality(frontierHit.place)}` : '')\n1430\t    : null;\n1431\t\n1432\t  return {\n1433\t    window_days: Number(days) || 0,\n1434\t    // Hits per day, so the card carries a sparkline in the same grammar as its\n1435\t    // neighbours without a second query.\n1436\t    last_hit: { ...lastHit, series: daily.map(d => ({ day: d.day, v: d.ok })) },\n1437\t\n1438\t    loop:     { state: loopState, since_sec: since, per_hour: perHour, basis: loopBasis,\n1439\t                series: daily.map(d => ({ day: d.day, v: d.att })) },\n1440\t    yield:    { state: yState, rate: cur.pct == null ? null : Math.round(cur.pct),\n1441\t                prev_rate: prev.pct == null ? null : Math.round(prev.pct), basis: yBasis,\n1442\t                series: daily.map(d => ({ day: d.day, v: d.att >= MIN_ATT ? Math.round(100 * d.ok / d.att) : null })) },\n1443\t    frontier: { state: fState, window_km: curKm == null ? null : Math.round(curKm * 10) / 10,\n1444\t                // best_km is window_km's correct name: it is the best distance\n1445\t                // achieved IN the time window, never a window measured in km —\n1446\t                // which is what discovery's own window_km means. Both are\n1447\t                // published until the board reads best_km; Domain 1 cannot\n1448\t                // change the browser in the same task.\n1449\t                best_km: curKm == null ? null : Math.round(curKm * 10) / 10,\n1450\t                // The card said \"this period\" without naming it. 0 = all time.\n1451\t                window_days: Number(days) > 0 ? Number(days) : 0,\n1452\t                // x-axis bounds, so the browser scales time without deciding\n1453\t                // what the window is.\n1454\t                from_ts: from, to_ts: now,\n1455\t                // NAMED `reach`, NOT `series`: frontier already carries a\n1456\t                // `series` (the daily sparkline, {day,v}) further down this same\n1457\t                // object literal, and a duplicate key silently takes the LAST\n1458\t                // value — so the first version of this shipped as the sparkline\n1459\t                // and I measured NaN km before spotting it.\n1460\t                // The chart: when we reached, and how far. See frontierSeries().\n1461\t                reach: frontierSeries(from),\n1462\t                // strip[] is the earlier distance-ruler payload, kept only until\n1463\t                // the card stops reading it. It answered the wrong question.\n1464\t                strip: frontierStrip(),\n1465\t                record_km: recordKm, flat_days: flatDays,\n1466\t                // `where` is the location line; `basis` stays the verdict. The\n1467\t                // card prints the km itself, so neither line repeats it.\n1468\t                where: fWhere,\n1469\t                basis: fBasis,\n1470\t                // The reached target, published as fields too so the panel can\n1471\t                // link or re-aim at it rather than re-parse the sentence.\n1472\t                at_name: frontierHit.name, at_bearing: frontierHit.bearing,\n1473\t                at_place: frontierHit.place, at_age_sec: frontierHit.age_sec,\n1474\t                series: daily.map(d => ({ day: d.day, v: d.km == null ? null : Math.round(d.km * 10) / 10 })) },\n1475\t  };\n1476\t}\n1477\t\n1478\texport function lastHeardMap() {\n1479\t  const m = new Map();\n1480\t  for (const r of db.prepare(`SELECT num, last_heard FROM nodes WHERE last_heard IS NOT NULL`).all()) {\n1481\t    m.set(r.num, r.last_heard);\n1482\t  }\n1483\t  return m;\n1484\t}\n1485\t\n1486\t// ─── CHART SERIES ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n1487\t//\n1488\t// Display aggregation, deliberately NOT inferences: the catalogue's rule is\n1489\t// \"small, and one thing\", and a chart series is something the system draws\n1490\t// rather than something it works out. See docs/OBSERVATORY_CHARTS.md.\n1491\t\n1492\t/** Attempts and answers per day. The 27 July cliff — ~1,000/day to 1, because\n1493\t *  traceroute.enabled was false — took a database query to find and should have\n1494\t *  taken one glance. */\n1495\t// ANALYSIS WINDOW. `days = 0` means ALL TIME and must omit the predicate entirely:\n1496\t// a naive `ts > now - 0` matches nothing and would render an empty page as though\n1497\t// there were no data. {"parentUuid":"272c7c0a-cfcb-48b9-9e26-ebe3dc47a2a4","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"4cc8afad-ad11-43a6-b5c0-6e6b0bd41ce6","agentId":"a2755cdc18585e64b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01ShoiPPrJWRX6HBGsBVpK6g","type":"tool_result","content":"const HAVERSINE_R_KM = 6371;\n/** Initial great-circle bearing, degrees from true north. Pure. */\nfunction bearingDeg(lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2) {\n  const rad = Math.PI / 180;\n  const y = Math.sin((lon2 - lon1) * rad) * Math.cos(lat2 * rad);\n  const x = Math.cos(lat1 * rad) * Math.sin(lat2 * rad)\n          - Math.sin(lat1 * rad) * Math.cos(lat2 * rad) * Math.cos((lon2 - lon1) * rad);\n  return (Math.atan2(y, x) / rad + 360) % 360;\n}\nfunction greatCircleKm(lat1, lon1, lat2, lon2) {\n  const rad = Math.PI / 180;\n  const dLat = (lat2 - lat1) * rad, dLon = (lon2 - lon1) * rad;\n  const a = Math.sin(dLat / 2) ** 2\n    + Math.cos(lat1 * rad) * Math.cos(lat2 * rad) * Math.sin(dLon / 2) ** 2;\n  return HAVERSINE_R_KM * 2 * Math.asin(Math.min(1, Math.sqrt(a)));\n}\n\nregisterInference({\n  key:  'reach.target',\n  deps: [],\n  mode: 'batch',\n  evidence: `\n    SELECT o.entity                                                   AS target,\n           COUNT(*)                                                   AS attempts,\n           SUM(CASE WHEN o.data ->> '$.status' = 'ok' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END) AS hits,\n           MAX(CASE WHEN o.data ->> '$.status' = 'ok' THEN o.ts END)  AS last_ok,\n           MAX(o.ts)                                                  AS last_attempt,\n           n.lat                                                      AS lat,\n           n.lon                                                      AS lon,\n           (SELECT CAST(value AS REAL) FROM config WHERE key = 'home.lat') AS home_lat,\n           (SELECT CAST(value AS REAL) FROM config WHERE key = 'home.lon') AS home_lon\n    FROM obs_v_traceroute o\n    LEFT JOIN nodes n ON n.num = CAST(o.entity AS INTEGER)\n    GROUP BY o.entity\n  `,\n  /** Pure. Rows in, facts out.\n   *\n   *  A TARGET WITH NO HIT IS STILL RECORDED, with verified:false and its attempt\n   *  count. MESH_REACH_SPEC §4: silence is censored data, so the stored fact is\n   *  \"verified reach >= X\", never \"unreachable\". 335 targets have never once\n   *  answered across 5,046 attempts and dropping them would hide the most\n   *  important number in the project.\n   *\n   *  A TARGET WITH NO POSITION GETS km:null, not 0. It was still reached; we\n   *  simply cannot say how far. Only 84 of 104 verified targets have a position,\n   *  so this is common and must not read as \"zero kilometres\". */\n  run(rows) {\n    return rows.map(r => {\n      const km = (r.lat != null && r.lon != null && r.home_lat != null && r.home_lon != null)\n        ? Math.round(greatCircleKm(r.home_lat, r.home_lon, r.lat, r.lon) * 10) / 10\n        : null;\n      const bearing = (r.lat != null && r.lon != null && r.home_lat != null && r.home_lon != null)\n        ? Math.round(bearingDeg(r.home_lat, r.home_lon, r.lat, r.lon))\n        : null;\n      return {\n        entity: String(r.target),\n        value: {\n          km,\n          // Bearing FROM US to the target, computed from stored positions. Not\n          // to be confused with the antenna bearing recorded on a reception\n          // (docs/modules/observations.md) — that one is a measurement, this is\n          // geometry. Keeping the two apart matters: one can verify the other.\n          bearing,\n          attempts:     r.attempts,\n          hits:         r.hits,\n          verified:     r.hits > 0,\n          last_ok:      r.last_ok ?? null,\n          last_attempt: r.last_attempt ?? null,\n          // §12: a self-reported position can be wrong, and a km headline will\n          // launder it into a record. EA1HTF claims 1003 km and arrives at\n          // -44 dBm. Flagged here so nothing downstream has to remember.\n          suspect: km != null && km > 200,\n        },\n        confidence: null,\n        evidence_count: r.attempts,\n      };\n    });\n  },\n});\n\n// ─── reach.ladder ───────────────────────────────────────────────────────────\n//\n// EVERY MOMENT THE FRONTIER MOVED. One fact, entity 'global', because a ladder\n// is a property of the whole record rather than of any node.\n//\n// It is the product's narrative (MESH_REACH_SPEC §1a) and it says something\n// uncomfortable that a single headline number hides: the whole climb from 5 km\n// to 95 km happened in about eighteen hours on 24 June, then 181.6 km the next\n// morning — and the frontier has moved 7.5 km in the five weeks since. A system\n// that iterates without learning plateaus, and this is the shape of the plateau.\n//\n// SUSPECT DISTANCES ARE EXCLUDED from the ladder, not merely flagged. A record\n// is a claim, and §12 is explicit that a km headline will launder a bad\n// self-reported position into one. A flag on a row nobody reads is not a guard.\nregisterInference({\n  key:  'reach.ladder',\n  deps: [],\n  mode: 'batch',\n  evidence: `\n    SELECT o.ts                                                       AS ts,\n           o.entity                                                   AS target,","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"d0a2b975-fe57-4aa1-883e-73c6fd3c5830","timestamp":"2026-08-04T10:27:47.854Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"272c7c0a-cfcb-48b9-9e26-ebe3dc47a2a4","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/usr/share/pac/dev/projects/mt-radar/node-dash","sessionId":"ce1ab9c9-df2f-4f79-a39d-d17da291a371","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"feat/alarm-transport-plugin","slug":"elegant-swimming-wirth"}

{"parentUuid":"01dd43fc-0eab-4a3d-8bbb-318863d077e8","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"4cc8afad-ad11-43a6-b5c0-6e6b0bd41ce6","agentId":"a2755cdc18585e64b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01HkicHMVriYGFHQT3RZxaog","type":"tool_result","content":"360\t//\n361\t// Called for EVERY packet, direct or relayed. That is the point — the nodes\n362\t// worth locating are the distant relayed ones, which _captureSignal below\n363\t// deliberately excludes (relayed RSSI describes the last hop, not the origin).\n364\tconst _packetObservers = [];\n365\texport function registerPacketObserver(fn) { _packetObservers.push(fn); }\n366\t\n367\tfunction _notifyPacketObservers(packet, device, ts, replay) {\n368\t  for (const fn of _packetObservers) {\n369\t    // ISOLATED ON PURPOSE. This runs on the hot path for every packet. Core\n370\t    // emitting into a plugin must never become core DEPENDING on the plugin\n371\t    // working — a throw in an observer must not take down packet ingestion.\n372\t    try { fn(packet, device, ts, replay); }\n373\t    catch (e) { console.error(`[persist] packet observer failed: ${e.message}`); }\n374\t  }\n375\t}\n376\t\n377\tfunction handlePacket(packet, device, ts, replay) {\n378\t  // ABOVE THE DECODED GATE ON PURPOSE. 15% of live packets are encrypted-only\n379\t  // (12 of 80 sampled 2026-08-03) and the early return below discards them —\n380\t  // but `from`, `relay_node`, `hop_start` and the receiving radio are all in the\n381\t  // UNENCRYPTED header. We do not need to read a packet to learn that one node\n382\t  // relayed another, and those relayed paths are exactly what the reach work\n383\t  // needs. This is also why it is not a registerPacketObserver: that hook fires\n384\t  // after the gate and would inherit the same blind spot.\n385\t  //\n386\t  // Isolated like _notifyPacketObservers: this runs for EVERY packet on the hot\n387\t  // path, and a throw here must never stop ingestion.\n388\t  try { captureRelayObservation(packet, device, ts); }\n389\t  catch (e) { console.error(`[relay-obs] capture failed: ${e.message}`); }\n390\t\n391\t  if (!packet?.decoded) return;\n392\t\n393\t  _notifyPacketObservers(packet, device, ts, replay);\n394\t\n395\t  // Envelope signal for every DIRECT packet, regardless of portnum — the\n396\t  // densest honest source of a node's own link quality over time.\n397\t  // hop_limit omitted means 0 (proto3 default-omission), so it is defaulted here\n398\t  // rather than making the whole computation null — see isDirectPacket.\n399\t  const pktDirect = isDirectPacket(packet);\n400\t  const pktHops = packet.hop_start != null\n401\t    ? {"parentUuid":"f04afff1-6f65-4b33-8799-2ac2f94592d3","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"4cc8afad-ad11-43a6-b5c0-6e6b0bd41ce6","agentId":"a2755cdc18585e64b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_012KPgKxDCV8bR2dSzY5YHve","type":"tool_result","content":"Confirmed live: 5 real nodes had `nodes.last_heard`/`updated_at` stuck at a\nvalue strictly older than the most recent direct-reception `signal_history`\nentry for that node (`signal_history` is written only from `hops===0`\npackets — unambiguous real reception) — up to 4.4 days stale.\n\nThe gate is on `upsertNode` alone (all 7 call sites in `persist.js` funnel\nthrough it), so every caller is protected uniformly regardless of whether\nthe stale source was a typed `node_info`/`node_update` replay or a raw\n`packet` event carrying mesh-gw's own `_replay:true` flag (also found during\ninvestigation — `handlePacket`'s NODEINFO_APP/POSITION_APP/TELEMETRY_APP\nbranches never checked that flag before this fix). Callers passing fresh\nwall-clock `last_heard` (direct reception, telemetry) are unaffected — the\ngate only ever rejects an incoming value that is *older* than what's stored.\n\n`nodeinfo` (below) has no `last_heard` column and cannot self-gate the same\nway — deliberately not given one (see `nodeinfo`'s own note) to avoid an\n`ALTER TABLE` migration, which this codebase has a documented incident with\n(see memory: sqlite-generated-column-migration-guard). Same rule for hop_limit, and for transport ('TRANSPORT_INTERNAL' = 0).\nsrc/db.js:251:    transport  TEXT    NOT NULL DEFAULT 'TRANSPORT_INTERNAL',\nsrc/db.js:1803:    (ts, from_num, relay_byte, rx_device, hop_start, hop_limit, transport, decoded)\nsrc/db.js:1805:    (@ts, @from_num, @relay_byte, @rx_device, @hop_start, @hop_limit, @transport, @decoded)\nsrc/inferences.js:371:// bridge it IN and then relay it to us over LoRa, and `transport_mechanism`\nsrc/mailer.js:29:  const transport = nm.createTransport({\nsrc/mailer.js:38:  await transport.sendMail({\nsrc/mailer.js:44:  transport.close();\n=== doors 140-215 ===\n  // and has never once arrived unrelayed — undoing the bootstrap fix for no gain.\n  // So they are kept as `legacy` and rank BELOW verified receptions rather than\n  // beside them.\n  for (const r of db.prepare(`SELECT num,\n                                     SUM(ts >= ${DIRECT_GATE_TS}) verified,\n                                     SUM(ts <  ${DIRECT_GATE_TS}) legacy,\n                                     MAX(ts) t\n                                FROM signal_history GROUP BY num`).all()) {\n    if (r.verified) bump(r.num, 'direct', r.verified);\n    if (r.legacy)   bump(r.num, 'legacy', r.legacy);\n    bump(r.num, 'heard', r.t);\n  }\n  for (const r of db.prepare(`SELECT from_num num, COUNT(*) c, MAX(ts) t\n                                FROM relay_observation WHERE relay_byte = 0\n                               GROUP BY from_num`).all()) {\n    bump(r.num, 'direct', r.c); bump(r.num, 'heard', r.t);\n  }\n\n  // ROLE, read once for every candidate. A CLIENT_MUTE is struck out entirely —\n  // it cannot relay by configuration, so admitting it would guarantee aiming at\n  // a node that can never carry us.\n  for (const num of [...cand.keys()]) {\n    const role = db.prepare('SELECT role FROM nodes WHERE num = ?').get(num)?.role || null;\n    if (role === 'CLIENT_MUTE') { cand.delete(num); continue; }\n    if (RELAY_ROLES.has(role)) cand.get(num).relay = 1;\n  }\n\n  // Strongest evidence wins the byte, OBSERVATION BEFORE DECLARATION:\n  //   1. times used as route[0] — it has actually carried us (keeps byte 4 on\n  //      !2d4cd04, 2039 routes against 10)\n  //   2. relay-capable role     — its declared function is to carry traffic\n  //   3. VERIFIED direct receptions — post-gate, we can definitely hear it\n  //   4. legacy direct receptions   — pre-gate, may have been relayed (B76)\n  //   5. most recently heard\n  // A node that has carried our traffic outranks one whose role field merely\n  // says it would.\n  const better = (a, b) => a.routed !== b.routed ? a.routed > b.routed\n                         : a.relay  !== b.relay  ? a.relay  > b.relay\n                         : a.direct !== b.direct ? a.direct > b.direct\n                         : a.legacy !== b.legacy ? a.legacy > b.legacy\n                         : a.heard  >  b.heard;\n  const out = new Map();\n  for (const [num, c] of cand) {\n    const b = num & 0xff, cur = out.get(b);\n    if (!cur || better(c, cur.c)) out.set(b, { num, c });\n  }\n  return new Map([...out].map(([b, v]) => [b, v.num]));\n}\n\n/** The door this target's packets REACH US through.\n *\n *  MeshPacket field 19 (`relay_node`), stored per received packet as\n *  `relay_byte` — populated even for nodes that have never answered a\n *  traceroute, which is exactly the population `knownDoor` cannot serve.\n *\n *  Direction caveat, deliberately not hidden: this is the INBOUND relay. The\n *  door we aim at is outbound and routing is 62% asymmetric, so this is\n *  evidence, not proof. Measured 83.0% micro / 71.7% macro against the\n *  dominant-door constant's 49.1% / 41.3% on the same rows, no leakage.\n */\nexport function relayDoor(num) {\n  const r = db.prepare(`\n    SELECT relay_byte, COUNT(*) c FROM relay_observation\n     WHERE from_num = ? AND relay_byte <> 0\n     GROUP BY relay_byte ORDER BY c DESC, relay_byte LIMIT 1`).get(num);\n  if (!r) return null;\n  const door = doorsByByte().get(r.relay_byte);\n  return door == null ? null : { num: door, byte: r.relay_byte, seen: r.c };\n}\n\n/** Where to point for this target, and whether pointing is worth anything.\n *\n *  Returns { az, door, source, km, needsBeam, why } or null when we cannot\n *  place a door at all — in which case the caller should NOT fall back to the\n *  target's bearing, because that is the behaviour being replaced.\n */","is_error":false}]},"uuid":"cfb39496-af3d-44e7-abb6-04d2bc98993a","timestamp":"2026-08-04T10:29:05.252Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"5c4f7d63-3c1e-4dd9-9398-52b02814b62e","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/usr/share/pac/dev/projects/mt-radar/node-dash","sessionId":"ce1ab9c9-df2f-4f79-a39d-d17da291a371","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"feat/alarm-transport-plugin","slug":"elegant-swimming-wirth"}

{"parentUuid":"59fc7274-b208-47f5-88f9-c3305d21c36b","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"4cc8afad-ad11-43a6-b5c0-6e6b0bd41ce6","agentId":"a2755cdc18585e64b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01HifVMqWUAKX7epPi2xa4KK","type":"tool_result","content":"1790\texport function listFavourites() {\n1791\t  return stmts.queryFavourites.all();\n1792\t}\n1793\t\n1794\texport function listFavouriteNodes() {\n1795\t  return stmts.queryFavouriteNodes.all();\n1796\t}\n1797\t\n1798\t// Task `packet-provenance-capture`. No dedup on purpose: two radios hearing one\n1799\t// packet is two observations of two different links, and both are wanted — the\n1800\t// same reasoning that made signal_history key on (num, packet_id, rx_device).\n1801\tconst _insertRelayObservation = db.prepare(`\n1802\t  INSERT INTO relay_observation\n1803\t    (ts, from_num, relay_byte, rx_device, hop_start, hop_limit, transport, decoded)\n1804\t  VALUES\n1805\t    (@ts, @from_num, @relay_byte, @rx_device, @hop_start, @hop_limit, @transport, @decoded)\n1806\t`);\n1807\tconst _pruneRelayObservations = db.prepare(`DELETE FROM relay_observation WHERE ts < ?`);\n1808\t\n1809\tconst _insertEventLog = db.prepare(`\n1810\t  INSERT INTO event_log (ts, kind, severity, summary, detail, source, probe_id)\n1811\t  VALUES (@ts, @kind, @severity, @summary, @detail, @source, @probe_id)\n1812\t`);\n1813\tconst _pruneEventLog = db.prepare(`DELETE FROM event_log WHERE ts < ?`);\n1814\t\n1815\texport function insertEventLog(entry) {\n1816\t  _insertEventLog.run({ severity: 'info', detail: null, source: null, probe_id: null, ...entry });\n1817\t}\n1818\t\n1819\t/** Newest first. The log page and the probe traces both read this. */\n1820\texport function recentEventLog(limit = 200) {\n1821\t  return db.prepare('SELECT * FROM event_log ORDER BY ts DESC, id DESC LIMIT ?').all(limit);\n1822\t}\n1823\t\n1824\t/** One probe's steps, oldest first — a trace reads forwards. */\n1825\texport function probeTrace(probeId) {\n1826\t  return db.prepare('SELECT * FROM event_log WHERE probe_id = ? ORDER BY id').all(probeId);\n1827\t}\n1828\t\n1829\t/** Interventions only, for marking the board's sparklines. */\n1830\texport function interventions(days = 30) {\n1831\t  return db.prepare(`\n1832\t    SELECT ts, kind, summary FROM event_log\n1833\t     WHERE kind IN ('config','deploy','mode') ${_windowSql(days, 'ts')}\n1834\t     ORDER BY ts`).all();\n1835\t}\n1836\t\n1837\texport function pruneEventLog(days) {\n1838\t  const cutoff = Math.floor(Date.now() / 1000) - days * 86400;\n1839\t  return _pruneEventLog.run(cutoff).changes;"}]},"uuid":"3e87b02f-5162-4338-ab93-3912a8837910","timestamp":"2026-08-04T10:29:15.293Z","sourceToolAssistantUUID":"59fc7274-b208-47f5-88f9-c3305d21c36b","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/usr/share/pac/dev/projects/mt-radar/node-dash","sessionId":"ce1ab9c9-df2f-4f79-a39d-d17da291a371","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"feat/alarm-transport-plugin","slug":"elegant-swimming-wirth"}

{"parentUuid":"629794ea-8557-4f35-9979-b3942a273ecd","isSidechain":true,"promptId":"4cc8afad-ad11-43a6-b5c0-6e6b0bd41ce6","agentId":"a2755cdc18585e64b","type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_0148BF93eD6GYqxUi5SLB2v1","type":"tool_result","content":"//\n// EVIDENCE STARTS FROM `nodes`, NOT FROM THE TRACEROUTE VIEW, and that is the\n// whole point. A LEFT JOIN is the difference between \"which of the targets we\n// have tried deserves another go\" and \"what have we never looked at\". Measured\n// 2026-08-02: 213 positioned nodes have NEVER been attempted once, 39 of them\n// beyond the 189.1 km record — while 423 attempts went to a single node with no\n// position that has never answered. The pool the prober never saw is where the\n// information is.\nregisterInference({\n  key:  'reach.mission',\n  deps: [],\n  mode: 'batch',\n  evidence: `\n    SELECT n.num                                                      AS target,\n           n.lat                                                      AS lat,\n           n.lon                                                      AS lon,\n           n.last_heard                                               AS last_heard,\n           COUNT(th.id)                                               AS attempts,\n           SUM(CASE WHEN th.status = 'ok' THEN 1 ELSE 0 END)          AS hits,\n           MAX(CASE WHEN th.status = 'ok' THEN th.ts END)             AS last_ok,\n           MAX(th.ts)                                                 AS last_attempt,\n           (SELECT CAST(value AS REAL) FROM config WHERE key = 'home.lat') AS home_lat,\n           (SELECT CAST(value AS REAL) FROM config WHERE key = 'home.lon') AS home_lon,\n           (SELECT MAX(strftime('%s','now'))) \t\t\t\t\t      AS now_ts,\n           -- SETTINGS ARRIVE AS EVIDENCE, NOT AS A CALL. This inference is pure\n           -- and the boundary test enforces it, so it cannot reach getConfig().\n           -- Same trick as home.lat above. COALESCE gives the default in SQL, so\n           -- an unset key can never produce a null that silently disables a rule.\n           COALESCE((SELECT value ->> '$.strategy'            FROM config WHERE key = 'discovery'), 'ladder') AS s_strategy,\n           COALESCE((SELECT value ->> '$.window_km'           FROM config WHERE key = 'discovery'), 25)   AS s_window_km,\n           COALESCE((SELECT value ->> '$.attempts_per_target' FROM config WHERE key = 'discovery'), 6)    AS s_attempts,\n           COALESCE((SELECT value ->> '$.cooldown_min'        FROM config WHERE key = 'discovery'), 30)   AS s_cooldown_min,\n           COALESCE((SELECT value ->> '$.max_silence_days'    FROM config WHERE key = 'discovery'), 14)   AS s_max_silence,\n           COALESCE((SELECT CAST(value AS INTEGER) FROM config WHERE key = 'observatory.window_days'), 30) AS s_window_days,\n           COALESCE((SELECT value ->> '$.proven_min_hits'     FROM config WHERE key = 'discovery'), 3)    AS s_proven_hits,\n           COALESCE((SELECT value ->> '$.proven_min_rate'     FROM config WHERE key = 'discovery'), 10)   AS s_proven_rate,\n           COALESCE((SELECT value ->> '$.new_node_hours'      FROM config WHERE key = 'discovery'), 48)   AS s_new_hours,\n           ni.first_heard                                             AS first_heard,\n           COALESCE((SELECT value ->> '$.mode'                FROM config WHERE key = 'discovery'), 'auto') AS s_mode,\n           (SELECT value ->> '$.pinned'                       FROM config WHERE key = 'discovery')        AS s_pinned,\n           -- The Observatory's OWN target flag. {"parentUuid":"d60a7d60-990d-4bba-977f-b1732a7d3d06","isSidechain":true,"agentId":"a2755cdc18585e64b","message":{"model":"claude-opus-5","id":"msg_011CdhZ7k45dme9wvT1FdNuy","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"text","text":"No `ReportFindings` tool is exposed in this session (searched the deferred-tool registry; only mcpp/playwright/monitor/webfetch tools are available). Findings are below.\n\n**Scope reviewed:** the observatory module's share of `origin/feat/alarm-transport-plugin...HEAD` — `src/observatory-ws.js` (+385), `src/relay-observation.js` (new), `public/app-observatory.js` (+478), `public/partials/tab-observatory.html` (+478). Verified assumptions against `src/db.js`, `src/hooks.js`, `src/ws-relay.js`, `src/config-api.js`, `src/inferences.js`, `src/doors.js`, `src/persist.js` and read-only queries on `data/node-dash.db`.\n\n### Findings\n\n- **`src/relay-observation.js:49` — HIGH. `captureRelayObservation` ignores mesh-gw's `_replay` flag, so replayed packets are re-inserted as fresh observations.** `persist.js:220` passes `!!_replay` into `handlePacket`, and `handlePacket:388` calls `captureRelayObservation(packet, device, ts)` without it. mesh-gw seeds packet events from REST on boot (`docs/modules/persist.md:53`), so every node-dash restart — and this service runs under PM2 with watch, restarting on every source edit — re-records the same packets. The row carries no `packet_id`, so dedup is impossible after the fact; the `_insertRelayObservation` comment (`db.js:1798`) cites `signal_history`'s `(num, packet_id, rx_device)` key as precedent while omitting the key itself. Live DB already holds **1,111 exact-duplicate rows** on `(ts, from_num, relay_byte, rx_device, hop_start, hop_limit)` out of 39,264. This is not cosmetic: `doors.js:152` counts `relay_byte = 0` rows as *direct reception* evidence and `doors.js:202` ranks doors by `COUNT(*)` per `relay_byte` — inflated counts change which node the rotator is aimed at.\n\n- **`src/observatory-ws.js:723` — HIGH. `contribution()` applies no plausibility ceiling to computed distance, so one laundered position flips the headline verdict to \"Gaining ground\".** Every sibling path filters: `nodeTable` at 400 km (`:640`), `boardVerdicts`/`answerRateByDistance` at 250 km, mission selection at `ceiling_km`. `placed` here is unfiltered, so `beyond = placed.filter(r => r.km > record).length` counts any first-ever completed route to a node with a bogus self-reported position (§12's \"EA1HTF claims 1003 km\"). The DB currently holds **128 placed nodes beyond 250 km** and 0 answered ones — the moment one answers once, the CONTRIBUTION band renders green \"Gaining ground · 1 new route past the 189.1 km record\", which is the exact laundering §12 exists to prevent. `furthest_km`/`median_km` are drawn from the same unfiltered list.\n\n- **`src/observatory-ws.js:665` — MEDIUM. `role_tone` emits `'gold'` and `'dim'`, neither of which exists in the browser's `TONE` map, so every role badge in the new Leads table renders identically.** `public/app-helpers.js:19` defines only `success|info|warning|error|muted`, and `toneCls` falls back to `TONE.muted` for anything else. `tab-observatory.html:120` binds `:class=\"toneCls(n.role_tone)\"`, so a declared ROUTER and a plain CLIENT both paint `text-base-content/30`. The router/non-router distinction this field was added to carry (\"repeaters are gold dust to us\") is silently lost on screen. Note the Doors table on the same page avoids this by special-casing `is_router` directly.\n\n- **`src/observatory-ws.js:83` — MEDIUM. The settings listener re-runs only `reach.ladder` and `reach.heard`, but `reach.mission` is window-dependent too.** `inferences.js:603` reads `observatory.window_days` in the `reach.mission` evidence query. After the operator clicks a new window, `charts.contribution.caps.*` — rendered by the \"why not further\" line at `tab-observatory.html:92` and read from `facts('reach.mission')` at `observatory-ws.js:741` — still shows the *previous* window's `window_km`/`skipped_beyond_window`/`skipped_silent` until the next 15-minute recompute. That panel is precisely what the operator is reading when they change the window, so the stated goal (\"a control whose effect appears a quarter of an hour later is not a control\") is not met for it.\n\n- **`src/observatory-ws.js:393` — MEDIUM. Third divergent default for `observatory.window_days`: `charts()` defaults to 7 while `config-api.js:32` defaults to 30 and `inferences.js:285/393/603` COALESCE to 30 in SQL.** The commit comment claims to have fixed exactly this (\"It was 30 here and in boardVerdicts while config held 7, so a missing key would have silently re-based the whole board\"), but only two of four sites were changed. With the key absent (fresh install, config reset), `charts()` computes `daily`/`silence`/`distance`/`bands`/`nodes`/`board` over 7 days and labels the payload `window_days: 7`, while `out.window_frontier_km` and `out.heard_km` — read from `reach.ladder`/`reach.heard`, which resolved 30 — are 30-day figures in the same object. That is the mixed-window bug the `observatory-window` task was opened to kill, still reachable.\n\n- **`public/app-observatory.js:722` — MEDIUM. `setObsWindow` uses raw `fetch` and never checks `res.ok`, so a rejected window change fails silently.** `fetch` only rejects on network error, so the 400 from `config-api.js:396` (or a 404 from an unknown key) resolves normally, the `catch` never fires, no toast appears, and no `charts` rebroadcast follows — the button simply does nothing and the user has no way to tell. The file already imports `fetchJSON` (which does throw on `!res.ok`, `app-helpers.js:61`) and uses it correctly in `obsPin` three methods above.\n\n- **`src/observatory-ws.js:757` — LOW. `contribution().verdict` prints literal `null` when the ladder record is absent.** `record` is null until `reach.ladder` has produced a fact; `beyond` is then null, `beyond === 0` is false, and the else branch yields `\"null new routes past the null km record\"`. `gaining` correctly guards with `beyond != null`, so the template paints the red \"Not gaining ground\" panel around the nonsense sentence. Hits on a fresh install and any time the ladder inference yields no fact.\n\n- **`public/partials/tab-observatory.html:75` — LOW. The contribution panel renders `\"null km\"` and `\"(null)\"` when nothing was reached in the window.** With no first-ever completed routes, `contribution()` returns `furthest_km`, `furthest_label`, `median_km` and `cost_per_new_route` all null (`observatory-ws.js:745-751`), but lines 75, 80 and 82 concatenate unconditionally — `x-text=\"charts.contribution.furthest_km + ' km'\"` → `\"null km\"`. Same for `caps.*` at lines 92-96 when the `reach.mission` global fact is missing (`?? {}` at `:741` yields undefined for every key → `\"window undefined km above a undefined km bar\"`). The rest of this page consistently guards with `?? '—'`.\n\n- **`src/relay-observation.js:23` — LOW. Retention is driven by an in-memory counter that resets on every restart, so under PM2 watch pruning may never run.** `PRUNE_EVERY = 5000` at ~20 packets/min is roughly a 4-hour interval, and `_sincePrune` is a module-level variable seeded at 0 on each boot. Any restart cadence faster than 5,000 packets means `pruneRelayObservations` is never called and the table grows past `relay_observation.retain_days` unbounded — the exact failure the comment claims a counter is immune to (\"A counter needs no scheduler and cannot drift\"). A cheap fix is to prune once at module load in addition to the counter.\n\n- **`public/partials/tab-observatory.html:546` — LOW. The hit-rate cell still divides `hits/attempts` in the browser although the server now serves `hit_rate`.** The class binding was migrated to `hit_rate_tone`, but the value binding was left as `Math.round((f.hits / f.attempts) * 100) + '%'`, so `reachModel()`'s new `hit_rate` field (`observatory-ws.js:266`) is dead payload and the page still computes a displayed number. No crash — `reach.target` attempts are a `COUNT(*)` and always ≥ 1 — but it is a surviving BROWSER_CONTRACT violation in the same table the commit was sweeping, and the two roundings can disagree (server `toFixed(1)` vs template `Math.round`).\n\n**Not flagged, checked and clean:** `obsSpark`'s `pts.indexOf(last)` (object identity holds through the reversed copy); the Leads `x-effect` pin loop (Alpine's reactivity does not re-fire on an unchanged `null`); `boardVerdicts` always returning all four cards the template dereferences; `_windowSql(0)` correctly meaning all-time; `transport` string vocabulary matching `standing-tasks.js` and `callout.js` consumers; `onEvent`/`registerWsWiring` single registration; `nodeTable`'s full-table DISTINCT scans (measured cheap — 39k/11k rows, both indexed)."}],"stop_reason":"end_turn","stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":2,"cache_creation_input_tokens":899,"cache_read_input_tokens":149778,"output_tokens":3738,"server_tool_use":{"web_search_requests":0,"web_fetch_requests":0},"service_tier":"standard","cache_creation":{"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0,"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":899},"inference_geo":"not_available","iterations":[{"input_tokens":2,"output_tokens":3738,"cache_read_input_tokens":149778,"cache_creation_input_tokens":899,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":899,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"type":"message"}],"speed":"standard"},"diagnostics":null},"requestId":"req_011CdhZ7iezcUQXCLby255tm","attributionSkill":"code-review","type":"assistant","uuid":"dfbdb1f0-07a9-40ca-8299-6c64337cefd0","timestamp":"2026-08-04T10:32:15.816Z","effort":"high","userType":"external","entrypoint":"cli","cwd":"/usr/share/pac/dev/projects/mt-radar/node-dash","sessionId":"ce1ab9c9-df2f-4f79-a39d-d17da291a371","version":"2.1.220","gitBranch":"feat/alarm-transport-plugin","slug":"elegant-swimming-wirth"}
